While we believe the mid-term election outcome is not a 
complete surprise to markets, particularly given pre-election 
polling and the recent weakness in both the S&P 500 and 
Nasdaq, the overnight correction highlights how complacent 
investors had become, with the VIX initially surging more than 
10% in pre-market trading, although down from intra-day 
highs after President Trump’s comments. 
As we have highlighted in our recent notes on the transition 
to mid-cycle, this phase is typically characterised by greater 
political headwinds. The mid-term election outcome 
reinforces the trend. The experience of 2015-2017, 
highlighted in the second table, offers a useful historical 
parallel. Despite the knee-jerk reaction, investors should not 
overreact based on one day’s moves. While we had trimmed 
equities to a core allocation last week, we continue to expect 
equities to deliver mid-to-high single-digit returns per annum 
during the mid-cycle, even amid political headwinds, 
supported by steady underlying earnings growth in both 2027 
and 2028. 
So, what should investors do to navigate the next few months 
before the newly elected representatives are sworn in and 
take control of the House and Senate? As we have been 
highlighting in recent notes, equity markets should still deliver 
positive returns, albeit at a more moderate pace. Hence, we 
recommend staying invested but diversification remains key 
to navigating the next phase of the cycle.  
FX: DXY near-term support at 100.7, 12-month target at 
98.5. A Democratic sweep potentially removes the legislative 
runway for further tariff escalation and for fiscal expansion, 
the two structural pillars of US exceptionalism that have 
anchored dollar demand in 2026. Reduced fiscal impulse 
compresses the growth and yield differential that has kept the 
US dollar well-bid all year. Additionally, potential Democratic 
restrictions on AI and data centre investment could weigh on 
US equity outperformance, which is likely to act as a 
secondary dollar-negative channel.

DXY headwind from a Democratic sweep, the magnitude will

Fixed Income: Still attractive all-in yields; prefer

In the past few weeks, the US Treasury yield curve has 
broadly shifted downwards, after our opportunistic call to go 
long 10-year Treasuries at the end of September. We are of 
the view the ultra-long end downshift in yields is overdone 
(given the still challenging US fiscal trajectory) and would 
recommend investors who had earlier taken long duration 
exposures at the 20 to 30-year parts of the curve reduce their 
positions now. We continue to believe the 3 to 7-year part of 
the curve offers the most optimal balance of income and 
interest rate risk, particularly via investment corporate bonds. 
Within corporate credit, we expect a potentially slower pace 
of AI data centre buildouts could marginally improve market 
technicals, as hyperscaler and tech sector bond issuance 
may slow (and reduce “crowding out” of other bond 
issuances). Conversely, from a credit perspective, we believe 
this dynamic may also make the data centre focused deals 
less viable, and would be cautious on some of this sector’s 
lower quality names. Meanwhile, less AI capex dependent 
issuers continue to offer solid credit profiles amidst the still 
strong economy. High yield corporate spreads have widened 
in recent weeks but dispersion within the complex is wide, 
with BBs and Bs credit spreads staying relatively resilient, 
while CCCs have widened to distressed levels. For income-
focused investors, we would recommend staying within the 
crossover range of between BBB-BB.  
Commodities: As noted in the opening section, we believe

prices could see a short-term spike, despite recent 
diplomatic overtures from Iran. However, for 2027 we 
continue to expect oil to stabilise at lower levels, as we 
think the Mideast situation should de-escalate given 
Democratic oversight of the defense budget, and the likely re-
configuration of oil supply chains. On the margin, we are also 
closely monitoring regulatory developments in the US shale

## Document 301: Capital Market Outlook (2026-11-09)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Capital Market Outlook (2026-11-09).pdf
Report source: Merrill Lynch (CMO)
Date: 2026-11-09

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 November 9, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Setting the Stage for 2027
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
Typically, a new administration sets the stage in their first year with stimulative policy
changes that play out in the second year after the new policies have been enacted into
law and begin to have their impact. This pattern shows up in historical data with the
average rate of gross domestic product (GDP) growth highest in the second year of

presidential terms compared to the other three years of the presidential term. At the
same time, equity market returns tend to be lowest on average in the second year of
presidential terms, which seems to be at odds with the fact that growth is strongest.
This apparent inconsistency can be attributed to the fact that the stock market is a
leading indicator that anticipates stronger growth and higher profits well before they
actually arrive. Once they do, as they have in 2026, the markets are looking ahead to the

inevitable slowing from the policy-induced peak in growth that typically arrives after the
midterm election year. The midterms also create uncertainty about the subsequent policy
environment which damps stocks ahead of the election only to be reversed on average
once the elections are over and the outlook is clarified. This phenomenon is seen in the
fact that on average the stock market has corrected about 20% ahead of midterms only
to rise about 30% from that bottom in the year after.

Q3 GDP data illustrate this pattern. Economic growth picked up through the first three
quarters of 2026 to a peak of 3.4%. Consumer spending growth averaged 3.8% over the
past six months, while the AI investment boom accelerated at a midteens growth rate.
Private sector purchases were growing at just shy of a 5% growth rate. GDP growth is
lower than that because imports of Asia produced technology-gear for the AI buildout
are subtracting a good bit from that underlying 5% domestic demand growth pace.

The new policies aimed at helping consumers through lower taxes and investment
incentives to spur private sector production have boosted growth as expected. Still, this
acceleration is passing, and the fiscal boost is likely to fade in 2027. Profits naturally
have followed this stronger GDP growth higher.
The upward trend in analysts' earnings revisions has started to roll over in our view, and,
while earnings are still growing, the rate of growth is likely to slow from here. In simple

terms, the second derivative of growth has turned negative. From way above trend
toward trend growth is still sufficient for the bull market to continue.
Another aspect of this transition to midcycle aside from the fading midterm
election boost from fiscal measures is the shift of monetary policy from maximum
accommodation to a more neutral policy. The Fed's rate cuts helped the midterm
acceleration in growth. Its shift to removing accommodation with rate hikes this year

adds to the odds of some momentum loss in 2027. That's already apparent as Federal
Reserve (Fed) Chair Warsh noted in his press conference after the September Federal
Open Market Committee meeting. Weaker balance sheet borrowers have seen their
spreads blow out as credit becomes more expensive. So far, the damage is confined to
the lowest-quality borrowers. Before, these spreads were unusually low reflecting easy
money conditions that are now normalizing. It's still early days in the new credit cycle.

Of course, how the market responds to this new Fed tightening cycle depends on how
aggressively the Fed raises rates. Analysts at Ned Davis Research note the S&P 500
has gained an average of 4.4% in the first year of all postwar tightening cycles. This
outcome varies considerably depending on the extent of the tightening. Aggressive
tightening cycles have seen an average decline of 3.6% a year later, whereas gradual
tightenings have seen a 10.5% average gain. In short, as one might expect, too much

tightening can cause a decline in stocks. Currently, the market seems to anticipate a
Investment Implications
Equities should continue to outperform
bonds in 2027 in our view, but
performance is likely to shift in favor of
higher-quality, less leveraged segments
of the market as financial conditions
tighten and earnings momentum slows,
while the accelerating momentum of
the early cycle transitions to midcycle
cruise control.

### Page 3

3 of 8 November 9, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
December and March nudge up in rates to reverse the easings by the Powell Fed. This
would be consistent with an ongoing equity bull market in 2027. Obviously, inflation
will have a lot to say about whether a mild or aggressive tightening cycle is called for.
With wage pressures benign, productivity strong and the early-cycle acceleration set to
peak, we believe the odds of an aggressive tightening cycle that derails the bull market

are low.
Other policy effects have shaped the current economic cycle. The shift toward more
nationalistic trade policies with tariffs, for example, has drastically impacted the pattern
of trade flows. U.S. imports from China have dropped from about 20% 10 years ago to
less than 10%, according to research from Strategas. Nevertheless, contrary to concerns
of a trade-induced recession, overall trade has continued to grow in line with the global
economy. Rather than deglobalization, the world is undergoing a reglobalization into new

trade spheres that better align with national interests.
According to McKinsey Quarterly, "The 2025 U.S. National Security Strategy, for
example, reinforces economic and supply chain security, industrial capacity, technological
leadership, and capital market strength as national priorities. China's latest five-year plan,
meanwhile, includes technological self-reliance, economic stability and the modernization
of core industries among its key pillars. In this environment, value creation is increasingly

shaped by new trade corridors, growing defense spending, expansion of export controls
and industrial policies, and shifting flows of foreign investment."1 These new trade
policies are an impetus for growth that is structural, helping to make for a strong
investment cycle.
Obviously, AI is a big spur for investment that has been facilitated by the new policies
such as accelerated depreciation of capital expenditures (capex). McKinsey Research
designates 18 arenas with cutting-edge industries they believe are driving the global

growth story for the next couple of decades. They note companies headquartered in
the U.S. and China account for about 90% of these arenas' market value today. U.S.
companies account for 75% of the market capitalization within the 18 arenas and
generate about 50% of revenues. Given its leading role in the arenas driving growth
into the future, we believe the U.S. is well poised to outperform as the world moves
into this new technological future. The 18 arenas are part of the economy growing by

several multiples of the old economy segments and help explain why U.S. Equities have a
persistent valuation premium over the rest of the world.
Because of the unusually rapid growth of these new technologies associated with the
singularity, while earnings growth may decelerate from its peak, there are structural
reasons to believe earnings are on a new, higher-growth trajectory and unlikely to revert
fully back to historical norms. The new technologies have much higher margins than the

old economy sectors. The combination of faster growth and higher margins bodes well
for the equity market as these new arenas of future economic growth become a bigger
part of the U.S. and global economies.
The midterms have changed views about the relative performance of some segments
of the economy. For example, strong sentiment to slow the buildout of AI data centers
was dramatically evident in the election results. Beneficiaries of the buildout are being

revalued to reflect this slower growth. Disapproval of the Iran War was also registered
in the results. Energy prices have dropped on hopes for a quicker end to the war. This in
turn has lowered inflation expectations and interest rates. Overall, while the midterms
have remixed relative performance between sectors they have not changed the outlook
for continued solid growth and an ongoing bull market in equities.
1 McKinsey Quarterly, Q3 2026, page 52.

### Page 4

4 of 8 November 9, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
China, the WTO and the Great Convergence No One Expected
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Twenty-five years ago this December, the West made one of the biggest economic bets
of the modern era: It brought China into the WTO, convinced that membership would
transform the communist giant into a more open and market-oriented economy.
The logic seemed impeccable at the time. The Soviet Union had long been vanquished.

American capitalism appeared triumphant. Free trade, deregulation, privatization and
unfettered cross-border flows of goods, services, capital and labor were widely viewed as
essential ingredients of economic prosperity. Globalization ruled.
Fold China into the rules-based system, the thinking went, and economic liberalization
would gradually pull Beijing toward Western norms. China would become a willing
participant in the U.S.-led global economic order. The world would change China, in

other words.
Not quite. At the dawn of the 25th anniversary of China’s WTO accession, the great irony is
not how China became more like the West. It is how the West became more like China.
In the U.S., and across Europe and Asia, governments are increasingly embracing the
elements of China’s economic playbook, which range from state subsidies to the promotion
of national champions to strategic industrial policies, among other initiatives antithetical to

the global economic norms that prevailed when China joined the WTO.
The deployment of trade tariffs, the subsidization of semiconductor production, the
promotion of domestic manufacturing, and the screening of foreign investment are no
longer hallmarks of China alone. They are increasingly features of economic policymaking
in the U.S., the one-time champion of globalization. The European Union, Japan, South
Korea and other nations that once carried the flag for free trade and open markets have

moved much in the same direction. Even the World Bank—a chief architect and guardian of
Western-led globalization—has come around to blessing the merits of industrial policy, a
strategy central to China’s economic ascent.
To be sure, Washington has not embraced China’s economic model wholesale. Neither have
Brussels, Tokyo or Seoul. The political, economic and institutional differences between
these systems and China’s remain vast. But the direction—set by China—is unmistakable.

It was not supposed to be this way. To gain admission to the WTO, China made significant
concessions over 15 years of negotiations. Beijing slashed tariffs on industrial goods and
agricultural products. Various sectors—manufacturing, distribution, telecommunications,
banking and autos—were gradually opened to foreign firms. Commercial laws and regulations
were amended or written, including rules governing intellectual property rights. Nontariff
barriers like quotas, import licenses, dual pricing and opaque industry standards were either

eliminated or phased out. State-owned enterprises were reformed and restructured.
These concessions—and more—were taken as signs that China was serious about
becoming a global stakeholder. It stoked optimism among senior U.S. officials that China
was inevitably bound to converge and embrace Western economic norms. And, for a while,
the bet seemed to pay off.
As China lowered trade and investment barriers at the beginning of the century, U.S. goods

exports soared. American firms poured capital into the mainland, attracted by an enormous
labor pool, lower production costs and what would soon become one of the largest
consumer markets in the world.
China’s massive labor pool-cum-ballooning middle class paid off handsomely for Corporate
America. U.S. affiliate income earned in China rose from just $1.6 billion (B) in 2001 to
nearly $9B by 2010, making China one of the most profitable markets in the world for U.S.

goods and services. American consumers, meanwhile, enjoyed the benefits of low-cost
China imports. And debt-laden America benefited as China recycled its excess savings into
U.S. Treasurys, helping to grease the financial wheels of America.
Investment Implications
We expect China will continue to rely
on exports and investment to drive
growth amid weak consumption and
domestic demand. Manufacturing-
heavy economies like Germany
remain particularly exposed,

reinforcing our neutral stance toward
Europe in portfolios.

### Page 5

5 of 8 November 9, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
But just as WTO accession helped boost China’s imports, it also supercharged China’s
export machine. Rather than shrink, as some optimists had predicted, America’s
merchandise trade deficit with China more than doubled between 2001 ($83.2B) and 2005
($202.8B) and continued rising thereafter. As the decade wore on, U.S. manufacturing jobs
and wages came under intense pressure owing to expanding China imports and the shift

in U.S. production to lower-cost China. Entire supply chains migrated across the Pacific,
decimating many manufacturing communities across the U.S.
China began producing and exporting at a scale the world had never experienced. In the
span of just a decade, China’s share of world manufacturing output soared from 8.7%
in 2000 to 18.1% in 2010, when the nation eclipsed the U.S. as the world’s largest
manufacturer of goods. Four years later, China was outproducing all of Europe—and has

never looked back (Exhibit 1A).
Today, China sits at the center of global manufacturing and trade, competing not simply in
textiles, toys and other low-value products but also in more technology-driven industries
like electric vehicles, drones, machinery and clean energy. China now accounts for roughly
28% of global manufacturing, according to the World Bank, well ahead of second place
America (17%). Meanwhile, the nation’s share of world exports—just 4% in 2001—now

stands at 15%, roughly on par with the combined market share of second-place America
and Germany (Exhibit 1B). The world is so awash with China goods that the nation is on
course to post another annual merchandise trade surplus in excess of $1 trillion.
Not surprisingly, China’s grip on world trade has significantly dimmed the brimming
optimism that greeted the nation’s WTO entry a quarter-century ago. Today, the consensus
is that China’s trade surplus has been aided and abetted by nonmarket practices and

policies that include forced technology transfers, currency manipulations, state subsidies
to industry, restrictions on foreign investment in strategic sectors, intellectual property
infringements and resource protectionism. Rather than dismantle its state-led, nonmarket
and mercantilist economy, China has tacked in the other direction, notably under the
leadership of Xi Jinping.
For investors, the implications have been profound. We now live in a world where

governments increasingly decide which industries are strategically indispensable, which
technologies must remain domestically controlled and which companies require public
financing. The “Commanding Heights”—or who controls the economy—have shifted
from the private sector to the public sector. In this world, investment returns are not only
determined by traditional metrics like earnings, the cost of capital and valuations but also
by geopolitics, energy costs, trade protectionism and national security. The world today is

far different from the world when China joined the WTO.
Suffice it to say that the 25th anniversary of China’s entry into the WTO won’t be met
with much celebration in the West. The consensus in 2001 was that the world would
change China. Twenty-five years later, it’s China that has changed the world. It’s the great
convergence no one saw coming.

### Page 6

6 of 8 November 9, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What are we reviewing to gauge the capital investment needed for the proposed Artificial
Intelligence buildout and the return required to cover its costs?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Officer
Capex by the 15 largest data center developers are expected to top $1.4 trillion in
2027, which is somewhere between 3.5% and 4% of U.S. GDP. A large portion of the
capital investment is earmarked for compute. The total capacity of gigawatts (GW)

under construction is around 34GW of Informaton Technology capacity, with the U.S.
accounting for approximately 70% of this, according to BloombergNEF. Given the
massive compute investment, we continue to analyze closely the total debt financing
announced, the approximate cost of capital of the capital providers, the associated
revenues needed to match or exceed cost estimates, and the individual credit spreads
(as well as credit default swaps spreads) of the larger hyperscalers and chip makers to

better assess the overall return on investment of the AI buildout.
BloombergNEF estimates, that at the beginning of this year, the highest-rated data
center bonds offered yields below 5%, which is now closer to around 7% today. In
addition, AI-tied firms have raised over $460B in debt financing this year, which is
more than double that from 2025. Finally, according to BloombergNEF and Bloomberg
Intelligence, as of September 18, 2026, it is estimated that the 15 largest publicly listed

data center companies would need to produce $2.5 trillion in revenues in 2032 in order
to achieve a return on invested capital equal to the company-level weighted average cost
of capital. This is about 5 times more than the expected revenue in 2026.
Recently, the concern across the capital markets is that there is not enough financing
capital to go around in order to fund the expected buildout, which is one of the primary
reasons why credit spreads have widened and yields have risen. We view this as a natural

development of such large-scale infrastructure transformations. This is the reason why
we continue to watch the overall health of balance sheets of the builders, the level of
profitability and margins, and the order backlogs/cancellations in the next few years. We
still believe we are closer to the middle of the AI buildout than to the end. Therefore, we
view larger-scale weakness in the higher-quality segments of the AI chain as potential
buying opportunities for investors who are underexposed to these areas.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

Bank of America, Merrill, their affiliates and advisors do not provide legal, tax or accounting advice. Clients should consult their legal and/or tax advisors before making any financial decisions.
This material does not take into account a client’s particular investment objectives, financial situations, or needs and is not intended as a recommendation, offer, or solicitation for the purchase
or sale of any security or investment strategy. Merrill offers a broad range of brokerage, investment advisory and other services. There are important differences between brokerage and

of America, Merrill or any affiliate to sell or provide, or a specific invitation for a consumer to apply for, any particular retail financial product or service that may be available.
The Chief Investment Office (“CIO”) provides thought leadership on wealth management, investment strategy and global markets; portfolio management solutions; due diligence; and solutions
oversight and data analytics. CIO viewpoints are developed for Bank of America Private Bank, a division of Bank of America, N.A., (“Bank of America”) and Merrill Lynch, Pierce, Fenner & Smith

The Global Wealth & Investment Management Investment Strategy Committee (“GWIM ISC”) is responsible for developing and coordinating recommendations for short-term and long-term
investment strategy and market views encompassing markets, economic indicators, asset classes and other market-related projections affecting GWIM.
BofA Global Research is research produced by BofA Securities, Inc. (“BofAS”) and/or one or more of its affiliates. BofAS is a registered broker-dealer, Member SIPC and wholly owned subsidiary

of Bank of America Corporation.
All recommendations must be considered in the context of an individual investor’s goals, time horizon, liquidity needs and risk tolerance. Not all recommendations will be in the best interest
of all investors.
Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

Poland will release industrial output, labour market and retail

show weaker consumer sentiment but a modest improvement
in business conﬁdence

Adam Antoniak and David Havrlant
Poland: Industrial weakness persists as
consumption loses momentum
October industrial output and labour market data will be released
on Monday. Fewer working days than in October 2025 mean that
we should expect low-single-digit annual growth in industrial
output, as the underlying trend remains lacklustre and uneven. Cost
pressures in industry are mounting, pushing producer prices (PPI)
higher. Recent readings suggest that price pressures not only stem
from rising reﬁning prices but are becoming increasingly broad-
based. Nevertheless, this has not translated into stronger pressure
on core consumer prices, as weak demand is forcing businesses to
compress margins.
Your privacy is important

### Page 2

The labour market has stabilised, with wage growth in the
enterprise sector slowing to below 6% year-on-year and
employment continuing to decline. With demand for labour
somewhat weaker, supply-side deterioration, reﬂecting the
shrinking working-age population, is playing an increasingly
dominant role. The unemployment rate remains low and among
the lowest in the European Union.
October retail sales data follow on Tuesday. Fewer trading days and
a high reference base for food sales will outweigh some recovery in

fuel sales following another cut in VAT and excise duty on gasoline.
We also see limited room for stronger sales of durable goods, which
have proved surprisingly resilient to the strain on household
ﬁnances from higher fuel bills and slowing wage growth.
Consumption is projected to weaken further in the coming quarters
as elevated uncertainty keeps the propensity to save high.
Czech Republic: Consumer conﬁdence likely
weakened on higher food prices

Consumer conﬁdence likely deteriorated in November, with
Tuesday's release expected to reﬂect the impact of rising food
prices. That said, the consumer mood likely remained solidly above
its long-term average, as households continue to beneﬁt from
decent nominal and real wage dynamics. In contrast, business
conﬁdence likely edged up in the same month, somewhat
correcting its previous pronounced losses while remaining below its
long-term average, pressured by rising input costs and ongoing

uncertainty.

## Document 347: GLOBAL MARKET CALL_ Stocks Are Cheaper Everywhere

Source PDF: all report pdf/GLOBAL MARKET CALL_ Stocks Are Cheaper Everywhere.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-22
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: GLOBAL MARKET CALL: Stocks Are Cheaper Everywhere
Date:    8 hours ago
GLOBAL MARKET CALL: Stocks Are Cheaper Everywhere

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
GLOBAL MARKET CALL:
Stocks Are Cheaper Everywhere
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 22 Nov 2026
View in browser
Photo by Gaël Gaborel - OrbisTerrae / Unsplash

### Page 2

The AI selloff went global last week. Investors fled to government bonds,
sending yields lower almost everywhere. Global profits are still booming,
yet valuations fell further. We see the selloff as a buying opportunity.
South Korean stocks are pricing in an earnings bust that analysts don't
see coming. In Japan, hotter inflation is raising the odds of a BOJ rate
hike. Here's more:
I. Stock Market Performance
Nearly every country ETF fell last week (chart). Taiwan was hit hardest,

down 23.2% in dollars, followed by South Korea down 13.7%. India was
the only gainer, up 2.8%. The US fell 8.6%, slightly less than the All
Country World ex-US ETF's 9.0% decline.
Stay Home and Go Global are now running neck and neck this year. The
ratios of the US MSCI to the All Country World ex-US MSCI, in dollars and
local currency, have been basically flat since the start of this year (chart).
On October 18, we decided to lower Go Global and raise Stay Home to

market weights.

### Page 3

The US MSCI's share of the All Country World MSCI's market cap edged
down to 65.1% from 65.3% last week (chart). Europe's share edged up to
13.7%, and the EM MSCI edged down to 11.1%. Japan's is just 4.9%.
II. Stock Market Earnings & Valuation
US and overseas forward earnings both remain at record highs (chart).
The global stock market selloff hasn't dented analysts' optimistic earnings
estimates so far.

### Page 4

Global profits are still booming. Forward profit margins are at or near
record highs almost everywhere (chart). Canada's is 17.5%, and the US's
is 16.9%. Japan's has climbed to 10.5%, rapidly approaching the EMU's
10.6%. Might profit margins be reflecting greater AI-driven productivity?
Maybe.
Valuations fell sharply again last week. The US MSCI's forward P/E is
down to 17.7 (chart). The EM MSCI trades at just 7.9, its lowest since the
Great Financial Crisis. We said valuations were at or near a bottom. They

went lower. We still think the panic selling will abate soon.

### Page 5

The US valuation premium is narrowing. The All Country World ex-US
MSCI's forward P/E is 11.2 (chart). The gap with the US has narrowed to
6.5 points from a peak of 9.5 in December 2024.
III. Bonds
Bonds rallied worldwide as investors fled stocks (chart). The US 10-year
Treasury yield fell 24bps to 4.92% from 5.16% a week earlier. The UK's
5.25% is still the highest in the group, followed by Australia's 4.96%.
Japan's yield eased to 2.78%.

### Page 6

Eurozone government bond yield spreads over German bunds are
widening again. The spread between French and German 10-year yields
rose to 157.4bps from 154.2bps (chart). Italy's spread is 110.3bps, and
Greece's is 95.9bps. France remains the weak link.
IV. Dollar
The dollar has been a safe haven during the AI selloff. The DXY rose to
103.8, up from an intraday low of 101.1 on November 4 (chart). We've
been expecting the dollar to remain above the lower end of its upward-

sloping channel. So far, so good.

### Page 7

V. Gold & Commodities
Gold's selling panic continues. The price fell to $3,555 per ounce on
Friday, its lowest level since September 2025 (chart). We still expect
support around $3,400. We pushed our $5,000 year-end 2026 target to
the end of 2027. This is not one of our high-conviction forecasts right now.
Commodity prices fell broadly last week (chart). Copper dropped 9.9% to
$5.48 a pound, as it continues to track the AI trade. Brent crude oil eased

to $92.57 a barrel.

### Page 8

VI. Focus On South Korea & Japan
South Korea's stock market has crashed even as its earnings continue to
soar. The South Korea MSCI's forward earnings is up 325.1% ytd, while
its stock price is up just 26.8% (chart). The price was up about 150% in
June. That has cut the forward P/E by 70.2% this year to a mere 3.2, the
lowest on record. Investors are betting analysts' estimates are too high
based on the reasonable concern that a slowdown in the AI buildout will

depress the memory chip prices of Samsung and SK Hynix.
South Korean exports tell a different story. They soared 93.9% y/y in
October to a record, led by shipments to China and the US (chart). We
see the US backlash against data centers as a speed bump, not a
roadblock, for Asia's chipmakers too.

### Page 9

Japan's inflation is running hot again. Headline CPI rose to 2.4% y/y in
October, above the Bank of Japan's 2.0% target (chart). The jump came
largely from the removal of gasoline subsidies.
The BOJ's policy rate is just 1.25% (chart). The odds of a December hike
are around 65%.

### Page 10

Japan's stock market has moved the same way as Korea's, just less
dramatically. Japan's MSCI forward earnings is up 31.3% ytd, but its stock
price is up just 6.6% (chart).
💡

log in to the Yardeni QuickTakes website and post them at the end
of the QuickTakes article. Paid members' contributions may be
featured in our segment, "Ed Answers Your Questions".

## Document 419: Three Questions About China’s Hidden Foreign Exchange Assets (2026-11-12)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Three Questions About China’s Hidden Foreign Exchange Assets (2026-11-12).pdf
Report source: CFR (Follow the Money)
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 17
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1
EXPERT TAKE FOLLOW THE MONEY
Three Questions About China’s Hidden Foreign
Exchange Assets
A trillion-dollar gap in foreign exchange purchases raises questions about where
China’s foreign currency assets are held.
Carlos Rawlins/Reuters
Articles Follow the Money Share
Log In
Regions Topics Research Experts Events The Future of American Strategy

### Page 2

2
PUBLISHED
November 11, 2026 8:32 p.m.
Brad W. Setser CFR EXPERT
Whitney Shepardson Senior Fellow
The PBOC argues—unpersuasively, I think—that it
doesn’t have any hidden foreign exchange reserves. I
would reframe that as hidden foreign currency assets,
as foreign currency lent out (or swapped out) with
domestic financial institutions is a classic way of
masking reserves.
Many in Asia—and some in the IMF—are inclined
to believe the PBOC. Fair enough, it is hard to

challenge an institution as large and powerful (in
some ways) as the PBOC.

### Page 3

3
But to convince, the PBOC really needs to provide
answers to three questions.
1. What explains the current gap between foreign
exchange settlement and the PBOC’s reserves?
Settlement is the purchase of foreign exchange in the
market by the PBOC and the state banks. It
technically implies taking an open foreign currency
position, as the foreign exchange is being bought with
renminbi.
Historically, it correlated with changes in the PBOC’s
balance sheet.

And some gaps before 2012 can be explained. In late
2005 and 2006, the PBOC did over $100 billion in
swaps with the state banks, who used the FX to buy
foreign bonds (portfolio debt outflows surged). Those

### Page 4

4
swaps were unwound after the global financial crisis,
and actually provided some of the foreign currency
that “funded” the China Investment Corporation
when it initially scaled up.
And in 2007 and 2008, the PBOC required that the
state banks hold FX against their domestic RMB
deposits (to keep the FX out of reserves; it was held
on deposit at the PBOC and shows up as “other
foreign assets” on the PBOC’s balance sheet and as
“other, other, short-term” in the balance of payments.*

After 2018, though, there are unexplained gaps
between the FX bought in settlement and the
PBOC’s balance sheet (technically cumulative
monthly changes to capture the fact that FX is held as
its initial CNY purchase price).
There was a net accumulation of FX outside the
PBOC’s balance sheet in 2020 and 2021, when the
CNY was under pressure to appreciate. There are
modest sales in 2023 and 2024 when there is
depreciation pressure. And there are large purchases

in 2025 and the first 9 months of 2026—total
purchases in settlement in the last 12 months (spot)
are $500 to 600 billion, while the increase in the
PBOC’s balance sheet is only around $50 billion over
the same period.

### Page 5

5
The gap over time is now $1 trillion. The PBOC
needs to be able to answer a simple question: who
holds this FX, and who holds the associated FX
market risk (given the FX has been purchased with
RMB).
The monthly pattern of growth in settlement strongly
suggests it is backdoor intervention; there are sales
when spot is weak (economically) relative to the fix,
and purchases when spot is either at the fix or just
stronger than the fix (the strong edge of the band

hasn’t been used). The pattern of purchases and sales
thus looks exactly like that of a central bank
maintaining a tight band around a (crawling) peg.
If the PBOC cannot credibly explain this gap, there
isn’t much more to discuss. The PBOC argues that it
hasn’t swapped FX with the state banks or put foreign
currency on deposit at the state banks. Fair enough.

### Page 6

6
But someone is buying in settlement; the PBOC
ought to know who…
2. Missing interest income
There is an obvious tell here—Bloomberg reported
back in 2022 that the PBOC transferred $158 billion
in accumulated interest income to the Ministry of
Finance as part of a special dividend to hold the
budget deficit down. Again, fair enough. But
absolutely nothing changed in the FX reserves held
on the PBOC’s balance sheet or in the balance of
payments.
That highlights a broader issue. Since the end of

2016, the PBOC’s balance sheet FX hasn’t really
changed.

### Page 7

7
Peer institutions generally have an upward slope to
their reserves as a result of “gains from reserve
management” or “reinvestment of interest income.” **
The Central Bank of China (Taipei) and Japan’s
Ministry of Finance are good examples.
The interest on ~$3 trillion in reserves should be
around $100 billion a year. A bit less during the “low-
for-long” era before the pandemic, when SAFE wasn’t
getting much income out of its euro portfolio. A bit

more now, when U.S. short-term rates are well above 3
percent and SAFE’s legacy agencies carry yields of 5
or 6 percent. $100 billion over 10 years is real money
—roughly a trillion dollars.
If SAFE has steadily been selling its interest income,
it should say so (the fact that BOP reserves have often
diverged from the PBOC balance sheet suggests a
more complex story).

### Page 8

8
Moreover, if SAFE has been transferring the FX to
one of China’s many sovereign wealth funds
(including those controlled by SAFE) or the policy
banks, it should say so.
But SAFE cannot say what you see is what we really
have if it cannot explain how it handles the interest
on its now $3.5 trillion in reserves.
3. The accounting for the PBOC’s entrusted loans
and other foreign currency claims on domestic
financial institutions, notably the policy banks

FX handed over to a sovereign wealth fund to manage
doesn’t appear in reserves. It isn’t a liquid reserve asset.
But it should appear elsewhere in the balance of
payments as either portfolio debt or portfolio equity.
And FX handed over to the policy banks through
mechanisms such as entrusted loans (or a simple
deposit or swap) doesn’t count as a foreign reserve—
the foreign currency claim is on a domestic financial
institution, and that isn’t considered a “foreign” asset.

There is no doubt that the PBOC has domestic
claims on China’s financial institutions. The use of
“entrusted” loans to fund the policy banks in 2010
was reported by Caixin and the Financial Times.

### Page 9

9
$95 billion in entrusted loans were converted into
equity in the CDB and China Exim back in 2015
(giving SAFE majority equity stakes in both
institutions through its Buttonwood investment
vehicle).
And the SAFE’s 2020 annual report contained a ten-
year progress report on the “diversified use” of China’s
FX (basically, the non-reserve use) which indicated
that SAFE continued to provide entrusted loans to
small, medium, and large Chinese financial

institutions.
Now perhaps these instruments have been unwound
—the China Development Corporation (per Stephen
Green, a friend) has reduced its foreign currency
balance sheet over the last few years. But that should
lead the foreign currency to flow back to SAFE
absent another use—and that increase in FX isn’t
showing up in the PBOC’s reserves.
In other words, SAFE should provide a transparent
accounting of the FX that has been bought but
transferred to other financial institutions and any

ongoing foreign currency exposure to the domestic
banking system (including the policy banks).
These aren’t big asks. The settlement gap is big and
obvious, interest income is a known issue, and SAFE
itself has reported its use of entrusted loans in the
past. There is no issue around the past use of swaps

### Page 10

10
either. A substantial share of the banks’ foreign
currency assets are funded domestically. It should be
possible for SAFE to explain the role, if any, it has
played in this funding.
There is, of course, another issue around China’s
balance of payments: the growth in foreign currency
external assets funded out of domestic foreign
currency deposits.
China argues that the growing foreign assets of its
state banks are simply a natural part of a growing

domestic financial system. But there is more to the
story.
Onshore FX deposits have tended to rise when
economics say they should not, and fall when the
economics say they should (offshore FX deposits
behave as expected). In 2020 and 2021, FX deposits

### Page 11

11
rose even when there was more yield on RMB
deposits.
And RMB deposits have continued to rise in 2025
and 2026 even with the RMB’s appreciation
offsetting the higher dollar deposit rate. This isn’t an
accident: banks just raised the interest rate that they
pay on dollar deposits to encourage exporters NOT to
convert their export proceeds into RMB and thus
take pressure off the PBOC (per Reuters).
In other words, the pattern of the banks’ foreign

currency asset growth suggests that they are offsetting
appreciation pressure by bidding for FX (or receiving
it from other state actors), not that FX flows into the
banks during periods when onshore residents worry
about depreciation.

### Page 12

12
Right now, the “settlement gap” is the most pressing
issue. FX deposit growth in excess of FX loan growth
can explain FX outflows of around $150 billion in the
four quarters to Q3. The FX outflows implied by
settlement reached $600 billion over the last four
quarters. Together they account for the bulk of
China’s reported $825 billion plus (I estimate a Q4
2025 to Q3 2026 surplus of around $900 billion) in
outflows implied by the reported current account.***

### Page 13

13
It goes without saying that this matters because the
weak RMB is the central reason for China’s massive
export outperformance since 2023—and that export
outperformance has continued in 2026.****
* I have been doing this a long time; I actually do
know most of the historical quirks in China’s balance

### Page 14

14
of payments data.
** The MoF can count a portion of its unrepatriated
gains from accumulated interest on its large reserves
against the budget deficit, but since the actual
interest is held abroad, it still needs to borrow
domestically against those reserves (domestic JGB
issuance and foreign reserves both go up).
*** My estimate of a larger “true” current account
comes from moving gold imports over to the financial
account, and from an assumption that undercounting

of the reinvested earnings of funds held offshore
explains the obvious problem in the investment
income account.
**** The spike in imports in Q1 wasn’t entirely real.
China was slow to adjust its import price index for
the rise in semiconductor/chip prices. This inflated
both import and export volumes in Q1. The rise in
gold imports this year also registers in the import
volume series—though the rise is best understood as
“importing” a financial asset.

### Page 15

15
This work represents the views solely of the author(s). The
Council on Foreign Relations is an independent,
nonpartisan membership organization, think tank, and
publisher, and takes no institutional positions on matters
of policy.

## Document 456: 「関西の景気動向」2026年11月号 人手不足とAIの急速な発展のもとで急がれる関西の人材ミスマッチの解消（PDF：1257KB）

Source PDF: all report pdf/「関西の景気動向」2026年11月号　人手不足とAIの急速な発展のもとで急がれる関西の人材ミスマッチの解消（PDF：1257KB）.pdf
Report source: JRI
Date: 2026-11-16
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

2026 年 11 月 
株式会社 日本総合研究所 
調査部 関西経済研究センター 
http://www.jri.co.jp/report/medium/kansai/

１．景気の現状と展望 
 関西の景気は、緩やかな回復基調を維持しつつも、一部に弱い動きがみられる。企業部門では、世界的な
AI 関連需要の拡大が生産や輸出の持ち直しを支えている。設備投資は、⾧引く中東情勢の混乱の影響が懸
念される中でも、企業の収益計画が高めの水準を維持するもとで、拡大基調が続いている。一方、家計部門
では、所得環境の改善が続くものの物価高が重石となり、個人消費の回復ペースは緩慢にとどまっている。 
先行きを展望すると、米中間選挙での与党の敗北を受け、トランプ政権の通商政策における不透明感や
AI・データセンター関連投資を巡る不透明感が強まるようであれば、関西企業の設備投資意欲を減退させる恐
れがある。加えて、中東情勢の混乱を背景とした資源価格の高止まりについても、金利上昇局面における資金
調達コストの増加と相まって、企業収益の重石となる懸念がある。個人消費は、持続的な賃上げの動きが下支
えに寄与するものの、物価高による下押し圧力が根強く、回復ペースは引き続き緩慢にとどまる見通しである。

２．個別指標の動き 
（１） 輸出・生産動向 
関西の輸出額（円ベース）は増加している。国・地域別にみると、中国（含む香港）向けは足元の振れが
大きいものの、アジア（除く中国・香港）向けとともに、半導体等電子部品がけん引し、大幅に増加している。
品目別にみると、半導体等電子部品が含まれる電気機器の増勢が著しい。また、一般機械では半導体等製
造装置が押し上げに寄与している。金属及び同製品も先端材料としての需要増や、それに伴う市況の高騰を
背景に高水準で推移している。なお、WTO の定義に基づき、関西の輸出全体に占める AI 関連財の割合を
算出すると、2024～25 年の平均 23％から、足元で 30％強まで高まっており、世界的な AI 関連需要の拡
大が関西の輸出を押し上げている。

一方、輸出を数量面でみても、緩やかながら回復傾向にある。中国向けや米国向けは、単月の動きの振れ
が大きいが、基調としては底堅く推移している。一方、ASEAN 向けやアジア NIEs 向けは半導体等電子部品
の増加を主因に輸出金額は大きく増加しているが、輸出数量の回復は緩慢にとどまっている。

鉱工業生産は、振れを伴いながらも持ち直している。業種別にみると、世界的な AI 関連需要の拡大を受け
て半導体製造装置の増産が続く生産用機械工業が堅調に推移している。一方、電子部品・デバイス工業は
依然として低水準での推移が続いている。

（２） 企業収益・設備投資動向 
日本銀行大阪支店「短観」（９月調査、全規模・全産業）によると、関西企業全体の 2026 年度の経
常利益計画（全産業・全規模）は前年度比▲7.8％となった。資源価格の高止まりなどによる下押し圧力を
受けて減益見通しとなっているものの、コロナ禍前との比較では、引き続き高めの水準を維持している。

### Page 3

3
企業が高水準の収益を見込むもとで、関西企業は積極的な設備投資姿勢を維持している。日本銀行大
阪支店「短観」９月調査による 2026 年度の設備投資計画（全産業・全規模）は前年度比＋10.0％と、
過去（2010～2025 年度）の平均伸び率を上回る水準にある。企業規模別にみると、大企業が同＋
8.4％、中堅企業が同＋24.9％、中小企業が同＋2.4％と、いずれも増加する計画となっている。機材やソフ
トウェアの運用・調達コストの上昇などから名目の投資額は押し上げられやすい状況にあるとはいえ、中東情勢
や金利上昇といった懸念材料があるなかでも積極的な投資姿勢に変化はみられない。

（３） 雇用・所得動向 
関西の雇用環境は、人手不足を背景に良好な状態が続いている。雇用関連指標をみると、有効求人倍率
は１倍を超える水準を維持しており、完全失業率（季節調整値）は概ね 2％台後半の低位で推移している。
雇用者数は高めの水準を保っている。 
所得面をみると、府県別「毎月勤労統計調査」から推計した 7～８月の関西の一人当たり現金給与総額
は前年同期比＋4.2％と、高めの伸びを維持している。雇用者報酬を実質ベースでみると、物価高による下押
し圧力が強まるなか、7～8 月は前年同期比＋2.1％と、前期に比べ伸びが鈍化した。消費者物価は、政府

4
のエネルギ―価格の高騰対策やコメ価格の落ち
着きなどが押し下げに作用する一方で、春先以降
の原油高の影響がラグを伴って幅広い品目に波
及している。中東情勢の混乱の⾧期化により原
油価格は依然として高水準にあり、先行き、物価
の伸びは拡大傾向が続く公算が大きい。実質所
得には下押し圧力が強い状況が続く見込みであ
る。

（４） 消費動向 
関西の小売販売額をみると、百貨店、スーパー、コンビニエンスストアの販売額は緩やかに増加している。 
需要側統計（家計調査）をみると、実質ベースでみた消費支出は振れを均してみれば横ばい圏で推移し
ている。財支出は春先に生じた自動車の税制変更に伴う振れの収束後、再び伸びが抑えられているほか、サー
ビス支出は一進一退の動きとなっている。物価高が重石となるもとで、総じてみれば消費の回復の動きは緩慢に
とどまっている。

消費者マインドは、中東情勢の混乱直後の大幅
な悪化から改善傾向にある。もっとも、個別の指標を
みると、「耐久消費財の買い時判断」は他の指標に
比べ回復が遅れている。物価上昇のもとで生活必需
品以外の支出に対する慎重な姿勢が根強く、物価
高が消費者マインドの重石となっている。 
先行きの個人消費は、賃上げの持続が下支えに
寄与するものの、消費者物価の伸びが再び拡大する
もとで、回復ペースは緩慢にとどまる公算が大きい。

（５）住宅・公共投資動向 
関西の新設住宅着工戸数は、振れの大
きい分譲マンションの動きを受けて上下してい
るものの、2026 年入り後、総じてみれば横
ばい圏の推移となっている。住宅需要は、金
利の上昇や建築費の高騰による下押し圧力
を受けているものの、現在のところその影響は
限定的なものにとどまっている。

2026 年 7～９月期の公共工事請負金
額は前年同期比▲3.0％と、振れを伴いなが
らもやや減少した。もっとも、国土強靱化やイン
フラ更新需要のほか、建設コストの上昇などを
背景に、公共工事請負金額は基調としては
堅調に推移している。

（６）倒産件数 
関西の企業倒産件数は、振れを伴いながらも、均してみるとコロナ禍前を上回る水準で推移している。原材
料費の高止まりや人件費の上昇などに加え、先行き、金利上昇による資金調達コストの上昇が収益の重石に
なると予想され、倒産件数は高止まりする可能性が高い。

### Page 6

（７）観光動向 
関西の外国人延べ宿泊者数は、コロナ禍前を上回って推移しているものの、中国人旅行者の落ち込みが続
いているほか、その他の国・地域からの旅行者も伸びが鈍化しており、インバウンド需要は全体として頭打ち感が
強い。日本人の延べ宿泊者数は、台風や⾧雨などの天候不順の影響もあり、コロナ禍前の水準を割り込んで
推移している。

３．トピック:人手不足と AI の急速な発展のもとで急がれる関西の人材ミスマッチの解消 
人手不足が続くなか労働参加率は各地で上昇しており、関西でも、2020 年代入り後、人口動態による就
業者数の押し下げを上回る就業率の上昇が労働供給を下支えしている。 
もっとも、関西では女性と高齢者の就業率は他地域に比べて依然として低い水準にある。首都圏に比べ若
年層の地域定着率が低いこと、地方圏に比べ自営業や家族従業者として働き続けやすい第一次産業のウエ
ートが相対的に小さいこと、といった要因があるものの、就労環境の整備などにより就業率をさらに引き上げる余
地がある。この点、女性や高齢者の労働市場への参加を促進することが人手不足に対する有力な方策の一つ
といえる。

### Page 7

7

一方、産業構造の変化に伴い、先行き労働力の需要と供給のミスマッチが拡大すると懸念される。経済産
業省の推計によれば、AI・ロボットの活用が進む2040年には、事務職を中心に過剰感が強まるのに対し、AI・
ロボット等の利活用を担う専門職や、IT 化や AI の進化では代替しづらい現場職の人材は不足する見通しで
ある。しかも、関西は都市圏として相応の労働力人口を有するため、ミスマッチの規模は相対的に大きなものと
なる可能性がある。 
関西では、産業構造の変化に即した人材の適材適所を図ることが重要となる。そのためには、リスキリングに
関する労働者や企業の取り組みに対する公的な支援の拡充や、企業内における職種転換を含む労働市場の
流動化を進めていくことが不可欠といえる。

《地緣風險衝擊中東信用基本面，但緩衝能力仍佳》
中東戰事持續外擴，除了美伊相互襲擊使得荷姆茲
海峽情勢依然緊張外，葉門胡塞武裝也對沙烏地阿

處高位不墜，布蘭特原油10月以來便維持在100美

但受到能源生產和運輸中斷衝擊，國際貨幣基金10
月下調2026年中東及中亞地區經濟成長率預估值1
個百分點至衰退，沙烏地阿拉伯更預估將衰退3.3%。

重點摘要 沙烏地阿拉伯(以下簡稱沙國)雖極力避免直接捲入
戰爭，但仍遭受衝突外溢效應波及。戰爭不僅導致
沙國大幅增加補貼，軍事和安全支出以及融資費用
上升均加大財政支出，因此，即便油價上漲抵消產
量下降的影響而使石油收入高於預期，沙國2Q26預
算赤字佔GDP比重仍達7.1%，遠高於年度目標的
3.3%。儘管近期公布的2027年預算計畫(PBS)目標
是將預算赤字佔GDP比率從2026年的4.9%(沙國官
方最新預估)縮小到2027年的3.6%，但若戰爭長時
間 延 續 ， 此 目 標 或 難 以 達 成 ， 目 前 估 計 2027 年
17420億里亞爾的債務目標亦將輕易被超越。不過
即使如此，沙國目前負債占GDP比重僅約35%，

2027年市場最悲觀預期也大約40%左右，仍是低於
全球新興國家均值約80%的水準。
中東另一主要發債國卡達對天然氣出口依賴度高，
天然氣出口受阻也使得國際機構普遍預估卡達2026
年經濟衰退10%以上、幾乎是2010年以來最重，所
幸卡達2026年以前累積巨額經常帳盈餘及外匯儲備
作為緩衝，即使在今年3月外部環境面臨極大壓力下，
卡達仍有199億里亞爾的經常帳盈餘，累積過去四
季經常帳占GDP比重仍達13.5%，現今外匯存底足
以彌補3月的貿易逆差，並可維持４年以上。這些數
據都顯示戰爭對中東國家的經濟及對外償債能力確
實已帶來短線壓力，但過去累積的龐大緩衝使其具
備良好因應能力，信用違約交換(CDS)目前並未因
定價油氣供應長期中斷而大幅攀升，今年雖持續發
債但債務壓力並不高，IMF更預期2027年中東國家
經濟將成長6.3%。然中東國家對能源出口依賴度仍
高，能源出口是否長期受阻則是後續觀察重點。

• 沙烏地阿拉伯負債占GDP比

不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行並不負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資之風險，並就投
資結果自行負責。未經本公司許可，本資料及訊息不得逕行抄錄、翻印或另作派發。本行以上市場資訊與分析，不涉及特定投資標的之建議。
重點摘要 《經濟強勁帶來的高利率無損新興債利差穩步收斂》
財政收支並非驅動新興主權信用利差的唯一因素，
總體經濟環境、市場流動性均牽動利差走勢，因此
雖然自7月以來沙烏地阿拉伯等中東國家信用利差又
再擴大，但也大概在3年均值附近並不算高。以歷史
經驗來看，BB級主權國家利差通常對財政惡化程度
反應較大，可能是因為該評等僅低於投資等級一級，
財政變化容易牽動投資人對升等至投資級的預期與
投資部位的調整，而在投資等級的主權國家中，則
以匈牙利和波蘭這些債務負擔較重的主權債利差較
易受財政問題影響。
油價和利率是近期2項重要觀察變數，也牽動利差走
勢，不過由於新興主權債指數中約5成的組成為大宗
商品出口國、3成為石油出口國，反而能受惠油價上

漲，油價上漲時與利差走勢敏感度也較油價下跌時
低；然而，更重要的是高油價對通膨和政策應對的
二階影響，將導致高利率長時間延續的可能性並最
終衝擊實體經濟，進一步限制石油出口國利差收窄。
以長期趨勢來看，利率水準的絕對值高低不必然影
響新興債利差，利率走勢反而和信用利差呈現小幅
負相關，利率走升反映的若是強勁經濟成長而非能
源供給衝擊，此時往往能伴隨信用利差收窄，反而
需留意的是利率波動放大及利率的大幅重新定價，
這才是牽動利差波動加大的主因。目前總體經濟環
境部分或可與2004~2007年相比擬，當時在強勁成
長的背景下利率長時間在4%~5.3%高位震盪，但新
興主要貨幣主權債利差仍可收斂至134點(此波低點
189點)，若經濟成長能在現今高油價和高利率環境
下持續強勁，利差則可望保持穩定。
重點摘要
• BB級主權債及債務負擔沈重
的投資級主權債利差較容易
受財政問題干擾
• 新興主權債指數中約5成為石
油出口國，降低油價上漲對
利差走勢的敏感性
3
即使油價上升，新興債利差與油價也少見正相關

重點摘要 《景氣及企業獲利續成長，企業債信改善趨勢延續》
三大信評機構持續認可新興市場四大區域企業信用
評等的改善，3Q26新興市場企業債淨升等規模大幅
提高至760億美元，其中獲上調規模達到910億美元、
創下2025年第四季以來的最高水平，也已是連續第
5個季度改善。
3Q26新興市場4大區域評等調升數量均高於下調，
其中以亞洲改善動能最為顯著，主要貢獻來自於亞
洲金融業，全年升評/降評比率已來到1.8倍，逆轉
2019年降評多於升評的劣勢；拉美企業仍是新興市
場中違約率最高的區域，降評債券數量與其他區域
比也確實較高，但降評企業家數已是過去10年來最
低，加上阿根廷主權債信獲上調帶動企業債(主要是
國有企業)信評調升，拉美航空業者如南美航空集團

(LATAM Airlines)在油價維持高位環境下仍於3Q26
獲信評機構調升至BB+，在企業債信分化環境下仍
有亮點，因此第三季拉美企業淨評級上調規模達
370億美元，反而創下2025年第三季以來的最佳季
度表現，全年共有234家拉美企業信評得調升。新
歐中東地區債信今年算是少數債信惡化的區域，很
大部分是受到戰爭及高油價影響所致，不過奈及利
亞和南非等主權評級上調仍帶來正向效果，梯瓦製
藥(Teva)評等更是上調至投資等級，帶來了120億美
元的貢獻，部分抵消地緣政治風險帶來的影響。
強勁的獲利成長持續支持新興企業穩健基本面，有
限的違約率輔以韌性十足的總經環境、以及活躍但
不過度集中的初級市場，均是支持債信改善的原因，
預期在企業獲利仍看好的情境下，第四季企業債信
仍將朝正向發展。

不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行並不負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資之風險，並就投
資結果自行負責。未經本公司許可，本資料及訊息不得逕行抄錄、翻印或另作派發。本行以上市場資訊與分析，不涉及特定投資標的之建議。
重點摘要 《聯準會升息年，新興債雖有波動但正報酬機率高》
觀察過往聯準會升息年度，利率溫和走升機率相對
較高，新興債市雖然多會受到升息壓力而出現一波
跌勢，但全年度仍有較大機率收紅，1996年以來僅
有2018、2022這兩個升息年度新興債市全年收黑。
不過2022年新興債市重挫很大一部分原因來自於聯
準會在短短一年時間內將基準利率自0％升至4.25%，
這種猛爆性升息與目前所預設的風險管理式的溫和
升息環境有所不同；另外，2018年聯準會雖只升息
四碼，但土耳其及阿根廷因美元稀缺引發流動性風
險，這2國經常帳赤字占GDP比重當時均高於5%，
對外融資壓力上升的同時進而爆發金融危機。然而
現階段新興國家經常帳已普遍改善，土耳其經常帳

赤字已降至2%~3%，阿根廷更轉為順差，基本面
與2018年有很大的差異。若扣除這兩段期間，新興
債市在聯準會升息期間波動雖可能放大，但在貨幣
政策方向更趨明朗後，走勢便會止穩反彈，全年平
均報酬率仍有10%以上。
至於新興市場企業債部分，過去因升息所造成的跌
勢約是主權債的一半，主要是因為企業債發行天期
多較主權要來得短，加上該期間獲利動能強勁，因
此在利率上升時期表現往往相對主權債更具韌性。
儘管利率走勢仍存不確定性，但最終若能穩定在5%
左右，預估新興企業債全年總報酬仍可維持在3%，
除非未來出現更大的外部衝擊，如利率持續大幅上
升引發對經濟成長受損的擔憂，或AI驅動的成長模
式被重新評價，否則對新興債市的看法仍將維持正
向，新興主要貨幣主權債在這樣的大環境下，短線
雖相對承壓，但後續反彈力道也可望大於企業債。
• 新興主要貨幣主權債在聯準
會升息年度正報酬機率近8成
• 新興企業債相對抗跌，公債
利率若穩定在5%左右，全年
總報酬仍可維持在3%
5

## Document 509: ベッセント長官が日本の金融・財政政策を気にする理由

Source PDF: all report pdf/ベッセント長官が日本の金融・財政政策を気にする理由.pdf
Report source: MURC
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

2026 年 10 月 20 日

調査レポート

ベッセント長官が日本の金融・財政政策を気にする理由

～米国の国債市場が抱える潜在リスクと日本への示唆

調査部 研究主幹 中塚伸幸

〇米国のベッセント財務長官が日本の金融･財政政策に「注文」をつける背景には、日本の政策動向が米国の
国債市場に悪影響を及ぼしかねないとの懸念がある。 
〇そもそも米国の連邦政府の赤字は GDP の 6%程度で高止まりしており、フローの観点だけをとれば日本より
状況は厳しい。特に、利払費が歳出の約 14%を占め、大きな負担になっている。財政赤字に加えて、米国債
市場には調達の短期化、ヘッジファンドの保有拡大、民間起債との競合といったリスク要因もある。 
〇日本もいずれ利払費の増加に直面することが見込まれ、ベッセントの「正論」は傾聴に値するといえよう。

1. ベッセント長官は米国債市場への影響を懸念し、日本の政策に「注文」 
米国のベッセント財務長官は 9 月の G20 財務相・中央銀行総裁会議後の会見で「日本はリフレ政策をやめる
べきだ」と発言した。また、これに先立つ日銀･植田総裁との会談では「円安に対処するための断固とした市場･金
融政策を支持する」と伝えた、と米･財務省が公表している。さらに、ニューヨークタイムズ紙は、ベッセント長官が
5 月の片山財務大臣との会食の場で「日銀が利上げすれば円安を是正できる」と大臣に伝えたと報じている。 
米国政府が日本の経済政策に言及・干渉することは珍しいことではないが、ベッセント長官は日本の金融･財
政政策に「注文」をつけている。これは、「米国債のトップセールスマン」を自認する財務長官が、日本の政策動向
が米国債市場に悪影響を及ぼすことを強く懸念しているためと考えられる。それだけ米国の国債市場は不安定
化リスクを内包しているということであり、実際、10 年債の金利は年初の 4.2%程度から足元は 5.2%程度まで上昇
している。日本が円安是正のために米国債を売却するといったことは避けてもらいたく、日本はまずは自らの金
融・財政政策によって円安に対処せよ、ということであろう。 
ベッセントが日本の政策を気にする背景にある、米国債市場のリスク要因について、以下に整理する。 
2. 米国の連邦政府赤字は GDP 比 6%、日本よりも状況は厳しい 
米国の財政状況は厳しい。今月公表された CBO の速報値では、2026 会計年度（25 年 10 月～26 年 9 月、
以下、「年度」）の連邦政府赤字は約 2 兆ドル（1 ドル＝158 円換算で 316 兆円）と、前年比で 12%増加した（次
ページ図表 1）。名目 GDP に対する比率では、26 年度を含めここ数年 6%程度の高水準が続いている。歳出は
パンデミック対応で 20 年度と 21 年度に急増したあと、22 年度以降は縮小したが、24 年度以降は再び増加が続
く。歳入も増加してはいるが、歳出増には及ばず、赤字が拡大している（図表 2）。

### Page 2

歳入･歳出の定義が異なるため日米の比較は難しいが、米国に近づけるために日本の一般会計の歳入から公
債金と前年度剰余金受入を控除し、歳出から債務償還金を控除して収支尻を試算すると、24 年度決算ベースで
日本の中央政府（一般会計）の赤字幅は GDP の 3%程度とみられ、かつ、足もとで比率は改善傾向にある。米国
は GDP の 6%という高水準の赤字が続き、改善の兆しも見られず、日本以上に厳しい状況といえるだろう。

日米の中央政府の歳入・歳出構造の違いとしては、米･連邦政府の収支には日本では社会保障基金に相当
する収支が含まれる点もある。具体的には、歳入にはペイロール税（日本の社会保険料に相当）が含まれ、歳出
にはソーシャル･セキュリティ･ベネフィット（老齢年金給付等）、メディケア（高齢者医療保険給付等）などが含まれ
る。したがって、より平仄を合わせる形で比較するには、中央政府に地方政府と社会保障基金も加えた「一般政
府」ベースで日米を比較することが適切である。この点、IMF の直近の World Economic Outlook Database に
基づいて一般政府ベースの財政収支とグロスの政府債務を比較すると（図表 3、4）、政府債務の GDP 比率は日
本が圧倒的に高い一方で、GDP 比でみた財政赤字は米国のほうが日本より厳しい状況にあるといえる。

### Page 3

3. 米国は利払い負担が極めて大きい  
財政収支の悪化は主要先進国に共通する傾向であるが、特に米国の状況が厳しい理由として、利払費負担
が非常に大きいことがある。22 年以降の長期金利上昇に伴って利払費は急増しており、25 年度の利払費（Net 
interest）は歳出全体の 14%を占め、国防費よりもウエイトが高くなっている（図表 5）。利払費は 22 年度から 24
年度まで 3 年連続して前年比 30%以上の伸びであり、25 年度も 10%増、26 年度は若干定義が異なる Net 
interest on public debt の速報ベースで 11%増と、依然として高い伸びが続く。 
 日本の一般会計の利払費（決算ベース）は 24 年度まで 7～8 兆円でほとんど横ばいであるのに対し、米･連邦
政府歳出の利払費は急激に増加しており、大きな違いがある（図表 6）。これは、日本の場合は超低金利が長く続
き、その間に調達した債券のウエイトが高いため、債券残高で加重平均した実効金利が低い（25 年度末の加重
平均金利は 0.98%）のに対し、米国では金利水準自体が日本より高いうえに、22 年以降急激に長期金利が上昇
したことが影響している（米国債の加重平均金利は 26 年 9 月末で約 3.5%）。 
 ただし、日本の場合も、10 年債の市場金利はすでに 3%程度まで上昇しており、今後、これに伴う利払費の増
加は避けられない。24 年度までの決算ベースでは図表 6 のようにほぼ横ばいであるが、当初予算ベースでは利
払費は 24 年度の 9.7 兆円から 26 年度には 13.0 兆円へと 2 年間で 3 割以上増加しており、借り換えの進展に
伴ってさらに増加していくものと見込まれる。

### Page 4

4. 法人減税が歳入の伸びを抑制  
 米･連邦政府の歳入は相応に増加している。特に、歳入全体の半分を占める個人所得税は 25 年度に前年比
9%増、26 年度も同 7%増と、着実に伸びている。一方、法人所得税は、歳入全体に占めるウエイトは 1 割以下で
あるが、トランプ政権による減税（OBBB 法による一部設備投資の即時償却の恒久化等）により、26 年度は前年
比で 16%も減少している（25 年度も、24 年度には災害等による前年の延期納付分があったことを調整した上で
も、やはり減少）。即時償却によって投資が促進され、将来的に税収増となる可能性はあるが、少なくとも足元はト
ランプ政権の減税政策が歳入を抑えている面は否めない（図表 7、8）。なお、関税も、相互関税が違法とされ還
付となったことから、26 年度は前年比 11%の減少となった。

5. 米国債市場の潜在リスク ～期間の短期化とヘッジファンドによる保有の拡大  
 上述の財政収支そのものの悪化に加えて、米国の国債市場には潜在リスクとなる構造的特徴がある。一つは、
調達が相対的に短期化しており、借り換え対応のプレッシャーが相応に高まっていることである。米国債の平均
残存期間は 5 年 11 カ月と日本の普通国債の平均残存期間 9 年 5 カ月に比べて短い。また、米国債のうち 1 年
以内に償還される債券の比率は 26 年 9 月時点で 33%と、19 年の 27%から上昇しており（次ページ図表 9）、日
本の普通国債の 13%と比べても比率は高い。IMF も、こうした調達短期化の潜在的なリスクを今年 4 月の 4 条
報告で指摘している1。 
 もう一つはヘッジファンドによる保有が拡大していることで、状況によってはそれが市場を不安定化させる懸念
があると指摘されている。FRB は今年 6 月の論文で、国債全体のうちヘッジファンドが保有する比率は 23 年の
約 4.5%から 25 年 9 月には約 8.5%に拡大しており、ヘッジファンドはレバレッジをかけた裁定取引が多いため、
何らかのショックが起きた場合に市場の混乱を増幅させる可能性がある、と警鐘を鳴らしている2。

1 IMF, “United States: 2026 Article IV Consultation,” April 2026 
2 Phillip J. Monin, “Decomposing Hedge Funds’ U.S. Treasury Exposures,” FEDS Notes, June 22, 2026

### Page 5

6. 民間の投資適格社債との競合  
 米国債市場に影響を与える要因としては、民間の投資適格債の起債が増加していることも挙げられる。ハイパ
ースケーラーによるデータセンター建設資金の調達などにより、投資適格社債の発行額は 24 年以降に急増して
おり、26 年も第 3 四半期までの累計額で前年比 25%増と、すでに 25 年通年の実績と同水準に届いている（図
表 10）。こうした投資適格債は比較的安全な資産として米国債と競合するため、国債の需給を緩ませる要因にな
り得る。実際、FRB のウォーシュ議長は 9 月 FOMC 後の会見で、長期金利上昇の要因として、強い景気、地政
学的要因とともに、「資金調達の競争（competition for capital）」を挙げている。

7. 日本への示唆 ～「正論」は傾聴に値する 
 ベッセント長官の「注文」は、内政干渉ともいえるが、しかし正論である。背景にある米国の状況を整理したが、
米国側の事情を抜きにしても、日銀の利上げと財政規律の維持は円安是正の点から日本に必要なことと思われ
る。特に、財政運営においては、足もとはインフレによる税収増に加えて、過去の低利調達の恩恵に負う部分が
大きく、現状約 1%の実効金利はいずれ 3%程度に上昇する。そうなれば、利払費は今よりも 20 兆円膨らむ。さら
に、防衛費や社会保障費の拡大などが見込まれる中、来年度からの食品消費税減税の財源も明らかになってい
ない。ベッセント長官の「助言」は真摯に受け止めるべきであろう。

摘要：
11 ⽉ 20 ⽇，国家⾦融监督管理总局公布批复，同意筹建云南农村商业银⾏股份有限公司（以下
简称云南农商银⾏）。
这次合并涉及云南省农村信⽤社联合社（以下简称云南省联社），以及省内 122 家州（市）、县
（市、区）农信法⼈机构；
按照⽅案， 123 家涉改机构将以新设合并⽅式组建云南农商银⾏，新银⾏成⽴后，将依法承继这
些机构的业务、财产、债权债务及其他权利义务。
按云南农信披露的 2025 年末数据，全省农信系统资产规模超过 1.4 万亿元，存款和贷款余额合计
接近 2 万亿元，不良贷款余额和不良贷款率已连续六年下降，新银⾏的注册资本尚未披露。
云南此前曾在部分州市推进统⼀法⼈改⾰。
2022 年底，迪庆、怒江两家州级农商银⾏相继挂牌。 2024 年 11 ⽉，德宏州辖内 6 家农合机构新
设合并，组建了⼀家州级农商银⾏。普洱、昭通、⽂⼭也曾就筹建州市级农商银⾏，启动清产

核资和资产评估服务的采购招标。
2025 年 11 ⽉，云南省联社⼀⾏赴内蒙古农村商业银⾏股份有限公司（以下简称内蒙古农商银⾏）
调研，了解当地改⾰遇到的难点和应对措施。内蒙古农商银⾏当年 5 ⽉挂牌，以新设合并⽅式⼀
次整合了 121 家涉改机构，开业时注册资本为 580.17 亿元。
今年 1 ⽉ 5 ⽇，云南的省级整合⽅案通过联合公告对外明确。
公告显⽰， 123 家涉改机构已分别履⾏内部治理程序，通过了新设合并相关决议。整合范围由此
前的部分州市，扩⼤到省联社和全省涉改的州县农信法⼈机构。
省级农信改⾰有两种主要做法。
浙江、四川、江苏、江西、贵州等地组建省级农商联合银⾏，同时保留辖内机构的法⼈地位。
下⼀级法⼈设到哪⼀层，各地也有差别。贵州规划的是⼀家省级联合银⾏和 9 家市（州）农商银
⾏。
内蒙古、⽢肃、宁夏等地采⽤统⼀法⼈模式，将纳⼊整合范围的机构归⼊同⼀家省级银⾏。法
⼈统⼀后，资本配置和⻛险管理可以在全省统筹。各地具体怎么合，⼜有不同安排。
⽢肃分两步推进。今年 3 ⽉，⽢肃农村商业银⾏股份有限公司（以下简称⽢肃农商银⾏）以 66 家

涉改机构为基础新设合并，开业时注册资本为 449.19 亿元。 9 ⽉，⽢肃农商银⾏⼜获批吸收合并
24 家机构，并将其改建为分⽀机构。
宁夏由已有的宁夏⻩河农村商业银⾏股份有限公司（以下简称⻩河农商银⾏）直接吸收合并 19
家县市农商银⾏。吸收合并批复于 6 ⽉ 29 ⽇作出、 7 ⽉ 7 ⽇公布。 8 ⽉ 31 ⽇，监管部⻔集中公布这

## Document 576: 凱基投顧_2357 華碩_余昀澄_20261112

Source PDF: all report pdf/凱基投顧_2357 華碩_余昀澄_20261112.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

十一月 12, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 1

動態更新 筆電代工 ‧ 台灣

華碩 
(2357.TW/2357 TT)

獲利率亮眼；伺服器帶動 2027年營收成長 
重要訊息 
3Q26 EPS 達 29.13 元，優於凱基預估，因營利率優於預期。管理層表示
4Q26-2Q27 PC 需求將放緩，但 2027 年伺服器營收將強勁成長。 
評論及分析 
3Q26 獲利大幅優於預期。3Q26 EPS 29.13 元，優於凱基和市場預估
38%、29%，主因營利率優於預期。3Q26 品牌營收季增 7%，主因市占率
擴張使 PC 營收季增 19%，抵銷伺服器營收季減 11%之影響 (AI 專案遞延至
10 月)。品牌毛利率季減 0.6ppts 至 15.9%，但營利率季增 0.4ppts 至
8.5%，超前凱基預估，主因 PC 營收比重擴大使產品組合有利。 
4Q26 營收展望優於預期。華碩展望 4Q26 伺服器營收季增 100%，大幅優
於凱基預估，主因 AI 伺服器需求強勁，且部份專案由 9 月遞延至 4Q26。管
理層預期 4Q26 PC 營收季減 15%，符合季節性和凱基預期；零組件營收則
預期季減 10%。因伺服器展望優於預期，我們上修 4Q26 品牌營收至季增
21%。考量 PC 需求放緩與 AI 伺服器貢獻增加不利產品組合，凱基預估
4Q26 營利率季減至 5.8%。但考量營收預估上修，我們調升 4Q26 EPS 至
21.7 元，季減 26%，2026 年 EPS 亦上調至 89.66 元，年增 50%。  
2027 年伺服器營收強勁成長，但 PC 需求放緩。管理層展望 2027 年伺服器
營收年增高雙位數。華碩表示客戶 2027 年資料中心興建計畫目前無異動，
而公司持續分散客群，增加 neocloud、二三線 CSP、AI 實驗室與主權 AI 專
案以降低客戶推遲出貨的風險。公司 Neocloud 客戶 40-50%來自亞洲，歐洲
30%、美國 20-30%，而華碩持續新增歐美客戶。然而，公司預期 4Q26-
2Q27 PC 需求放緩，2027 年全球 PC 需求年減 10-15%，但目標華碩出貨優
於產業平均。因市場需求降溫，公司可能較難將零組件漲價完全轉嫁至系統
零售價，營利率恐承壓。我們下修 2027 年 PC 營收至年減 4%，但調升伺服
器營收至 5,760 億元，年增 54% (營收比重 48%)。儘管營收預估調升至年
增 19%，但考慮利息支出較高，維持 2027 年 EPS 82.51 元，年減 8%。 
投資建議 
我們將評價基準改為 2027 年獲利，上修目標價至 1,155 元，基於 14 倍
2027 年 EPS 預估 (前次為 14x 2026-27F EPS)，評等維持「增加持股」。 
投資風險 
筆電需求疲弱；AI GPU 供給緊缺；匯率波動。

十一月 12, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 5

五月 13, 2025 
ESG 圖表定義 
項目 定義 說明 
能源使用 
直接與間接能源消耗總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司運營範圍內消耗之能源總量 
- 能源使用總量 = 直接能源消耗總量 + 間接能源消耗量 
- 總能源使用量包括購買的能源、生產的能源 
- 就公用事業而言，輸電/電網損耗為其業務活動之一部分，被視為總能源消耗，數據不包括
為滿足能源使用而生產的電力（公用事業為出售而生產） 
- 就公用事業而言，用於能源生產的煤、天然氣或核能等原料不屬於「總能源使用」項下

購買之再生能源 
Primary 再生能源購買總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司各種來源之能源消耗量與購買的能源中屬於自然界可再生者（太陽能、風能、水能、
生物質能、地熱能）之量  
- 如無證據顯示再生能源由公司生產，則所報告的能源數據視為購買的再生能源

再生能源使用率 再生能源占總能源消耗量之比例  
CO2 約當排放量 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量(公噸) 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化合
物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 (NF3)

CO2 約當排放量營收比 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量（公噸）銷售（百萬元）比 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化合
物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 (NF3)

廢棄物總量 
廢棄物總量(公噸) 
- 總廢棄物 = 無害廢棄物 + 有害廢棄物 
- 僅考慮固體廢棄物，但如液體廢棄物以公噸為單位呈報，則會將其加入求得包含液體廢棄
物之總量 
- 對於採礦、石油與天然氣等行業，tailings、廢石、煤、飛灰等廢棄物亦考慮在內

廢棄物回收率 
公司呈報的廢棄物回收率 
- 廢棄物回收率 = 廢棄物回收量/總廢棄物*100 
- 廢棄物轉化為能源或經由廢棄物焚燒產生能源視為廢棄物回收 
- 經由堆肥回收的廢棄物視為回收之廢棄物

總取水量 
總取水量(立方公尺) 
- 由呈報組織直接或經水公司等中介機構從任何水源抽取的總水量 
- 水井、城鎮/公用事業/市政用水、河水、地表水等不同之水源均予以考慮

環保支出 
環保支出總金額 
- 所有用於環境保護，或防止、減少、控制環境因素、影響、危害的投資與支出，亦包括處
置、處理、衛生、清理支出

員工流動率 
員工流動率 
- 包括任何原因（自願或非自願）離開公司的員工，如辭職、退休、自然離職/死亡、醫療失
能、冗員、裁員、重組、解僱、裁減或定期合約到期 
- 員工流動率 =（離開之員工/平均員工人數）*100 
- 平均員工人數 =（本年末員工人數 + 去年末員工人數）/2 
- 本財年末員工數 =上個財年末員工數 + 新員工數 - 離開之員工數

女性經理 
女性經理百分比 
- 女性經理占公司經理的百分比 
- 如有不同階層之百分比，如最高階、高階、中階、初階，則會考慮中階女性經理之百分比 
- 女性經理百分比 = 女性經理人數/經理總數*100

女性員工 
女性員工百分比 
- 女性員工占公司員工總數的百分比 
- 女性員工百分比 = 女性人數/員工總數*100

教育訓練總時數 
所有員工教育訓練總時數 
- 僅考慮員工教育訓練時數 
- 包括一般員工所有類型的教育訓練（如健康與安全、環境、急難事件救援、技能與職業發
展） 
- 如果數據以天為單位，則乘以 8，係假設 1 天 =  8 小時工作

每位員工教育訓練時數 平均每年每位員工總教育訓練時數  
資料來源：Refinitiv；凱基

## Document 622: 匯率市場總評：美元漲勢將暫緩

Source PDF: all report pdf/匯率市場總評：美元漲勢將暫緩.pdf
Report source: Fubon
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

台北富邦銀行 
Oct 29, 2026 | 5:00 PM (GMT+8) 
匯率市場總評：美元漲勢將暫緩
投資研究部 
 Research 
Financial Market 
▎全球最大淨負債國，美元居主導地位 
美國 2026 年第 2 季國際投資部位(IIP)顯示，美國對外
資產 47 兆、負債 69.4 兆美元，淨國際投資部位(NIIP)
為負 22.4 兆美元，居全球最大淨負債國。而負債主要
集中於證券投資項，佔整體淨負債近 9 成，外資持有美
股及美債規模達 40.9 兆，全球資金持續流向美元資產。

美國直接投資部位接近平衡，證券投資方面，美國持有
高報酬海外股權與直接投資，分別佔資產比重約 35.8%、

圖 1 美國主要持有外國股票、直接投資

資料來源：2026/9/14，Bloomberg、投資研究部

32.4%(見圖 1)，對外負債則以股票及債券、直接投資為
主(見圖 2)，今年第 2 季投資組合淨部位為負 19.9 兆美
元(見圖 4)，全球資金持續配置美國金融資產，美股、
美債市場規模穩居全球之首，美元主導地位難以被撼動。

圖 2 外國主要持有美國股票、債券

資料來源：2026/9/14，Bloomberg、投資研究部

美國直接投資呈現雙向擴張趨勢，今年第 2 季美國對外
直接投資高達 1515 億美元，顯示美國企業在 AI 供應
鏈重組與全球產能布局需求下，再度加速海外投資，不

◼ 美國居全球最大淨負債國，證券投資淨負債佔比達 9 成，外資持有美股及美債規
模達 40.9 兆，全球資金除了流向美元資產，AI 資料中心與先進製造業擴廠潮帶動
下，全球資本也朝美國集中，今年第 2 季外國對美直接投資達 1873 億美元。

◼ 今年 9 月升息後，美國 10 年公債利率與政策利率差值擴大，市場預期美國經濟
仍具韌性，期中選舉後迎來消費旺季，年底前資金可望流入具獲利、估值相對合
理的新興亞股，美元將受到抑制，不過升息預期猶存下，美元將不時回升。 
◼ 雖然 AI 產業需求強勁，不過新雲端服務業者面臨籌資難度加大問題，隨著資金成
本提升，市場將更謹慎關注 AI 產業的高成長、能否順利轉化為自由現金流，一旦
科技巨頭資本支出放緩、企業投資變現速度不如預期，或新創企業融資斷鏈等，
多重風險恐加劇美股震盪，預計避險情緒升溫將驅動美元走強。

### Page 2

2

誠信親切、專業創新 週報

圖 3 2026/2Q 美國居 NIIP 最大淨負債國 
資料來源：2026/10/1，Bloomberg、投資研究部

圖 4 外資持續投入美國金融資產

資料來源：2026/10/5，Bloomberg、投資研究部

圖 5 美國直接投資呈雙向擴張

資料來源：2026/10/27，Bloomberg、投資研究部

過美國金融帳對外直接投資負債也達 1873 億美元，創
近年高點(見圖 5)，包含來自台積電、三星等半導體投
資，AI 資料中心、電池與先進製造業擴廠潮，加上美國
政府推動製造業回流，全球資本也持續向美國集中，今
年前 2 季直接投資淨流入美國，中長期將支撐美元。

▎景氣仍具韌性，美德利差將支撐美元  
10 月 FOMC 維持政策利率，通膨仍然偏高，聯準會需
累積更多資訊，以判斷適當的貨幣政策，確保通膨及時
回到 2％，10/29 市場預期 12 月將升息 1 碼機率約
84%，明年將再升息 2 碼，自今年 9 月升息以來將累計
升息 4 碼，預計將支撐美國 10 年公債利率於高檔波動。

自 1990 年以來，美國 10 年公債利率與 Fed Fund Rate
差值處於負 1.6%到 4%區間(見圖 6)，而在步入升息循
環後，兩者差值大多呈現縮窄，包含 1994/2、2004/6、
2016/12、2022/3，反映持續升息對景氣具抑制效果。

1999/6 啟動升息循環後，美 10 年公債利率與政策利
率差值持續擴大、於 2000/1 才自波段高位反轉向下，
當時網際網路革命帶動生產力大幅提升，企業投資與股
市熱絡，市場預期未來經濟將高速成長，S&P 500 指數
持續創高、直到 2000/3/24 網路泡沫才破滅。而今年 9
月升息後，美 10 年公債利率與政策利率差值擴大，反
映目前政策利率環境下，市場預期美國經濟仍具韌性。

圖 6 近期美 10 年公債利率、政策利率差值擴大  
資料來源：2026/10/13，Bloomberg、投資研究部

近期美德實質利差自高檔回落抑制美元(見圖 7)，不過
歐元區景氣相對美國疲弱，此外，法國政府面臨預算協
商壓力，由於國會呈現分裂，政府難以推動數百億歐元
的財政整頓計畫，市場擔憂法國赤字與債務問題恐持續
惡化，帶動法國公債利率上升、法德公債利差擴大，市
場對歐元區財政紀律信心轉弱，對歐元形成壓力，而明
年 4 月法國將迎來總統大選，政治不確定性升溫，將可
能進一步抑制歐元。

圖 7 美德實質利差自波段高位回落

資料來源：2026/10/29，Bloomberg、投資研究部

▎資金成本升溫，美股震盪將支撐美元 
英特爾、博通執行長不約而同指出，資金取得管道可能
超越晶片供應，成為限制 AI 擴張的重大因素，而輝達
黃仁勳也表示，AI 產業需求強勁，新雲端服務業者
(neocloud)資金短缺、籌資難度加大。近期 CoreWeave
債券價格、股價雙雙暴跌(見圖 8)，反映出市場對其高
槓桿擴張模式的疑慮，隨著資金成本提升，市場將更謹
慎關注 AI 產業的高成長、能否順利轉化為自由現金流， 
AI 新創 Anthropic 將於 11 月中下旬上市，儘管第 3 季
營收達 168 億美元，不過季增率由從 147%放緩至 45%，
市場對其 1.5 兆到 2 兆美元 IPO 估值保持高度警覺。

### Page 4

誠信親切、專業創新 週報

圖 9 美債利率與美股預估本益比走勢 
資料來源：2026/10/15，Bloomberg、投資研究部

理論上高利率抑制美股估值下，美國 10 年期公債利率
與 S&P 500 預估本益比呈現負向關係，然而 1999 到
2000 年科技泡沫及 2023 年以來 AI 行情期間，市場對
企業獲利成長的樂觀預期明顯超越利率因素，使美債利
率與估值同步攀升(見圖 9)。10/29 美國 10 年期公債利
率自波段高位小幅回落至 5%附近，S&P 500 預估本益
比維持 21 倍以上，顯示市場對 AI 驅動的成長前景仍抱
持樂觀預期，若未來聯準會持續升息、企業獲利表現未
能持續驗證成長，高估值恐面臨修正壓力。

圖 10  投機者美元期貨淨多單逼近歷史高位

資料來源：2026/10/27，Bloomberg、投資研究部

從期貨籌碼面來看，10/16 當週 CFTC 主要貨幣兌美元
投機者淨空單逼近歷史高位(見圖 10)，中長線而言，進
一步看多美元空間將受限。期中選舉後將迎來聖誕行情，
全球 AI 需求續強，年底前資金可望流入具獲利、估值
相對合理新興亞股(見圖 11)、美元也將受抑制。儘管明
年 Q2 油價將受高基期影響，然而近期油價不時彈升，
也令通膨回落受阻，高利率環境下，市場將謹慎看待 AI
投資報酬與融資風險，一旦科技巨頭資本支出放緩、企
業投資變現速度不如預期，或新創企業融資斷鏈等，多
重風險恐加劇美股震盪，避險情緒將驅動美元走強。

### Page 3

3
AI意猶未盡．後退原來是前進
投
資
策
略
市場展望投資策略
 股債並進，後退將化為前進動能 :  AI基建需求仍強，近
期股價回檔主要反映市場對政策、資本支出及估值的重
新定價，而非產業趨勢反轉。建議均衡配置，核心聚焦
大型科技股與具AI供應鏈優勢的台灣、韓國，鎖定半導
體、雲端服務與高效能運算。衛星防禦以債券為主，搭
配防守型類股或透過分散投資降波動。
 配置較高信評債券，避免追逐低信評收益：信評選擇聚
焦高品質信用債，增配 A級以上投資等級公司債，優先
布局現金流穩健、具市場領導地位的大型企業，降低景
氣循環與再融資風險，避免過度追逐低信評債券收益。
(Page 10、11)
 科技股回檔可分批低接：科技股因市場擔憂龐大資本支
出對現金流的壓力，但資料中心政策方向未改變，可在
估值回檔時分批逢低布局具備商業變現能力企業。衛星
部 位 可 搭 配 工 業 、 非 必 須 消 費 ， 提 升 投 組 穩 定 度 。
(詳見Page 14、15、16)
 匯市展望：美國經濟成長與利率水準仍優，美元短期持

續受到利差優勢支撐。期中選舉落幕，政策不確定性下
降，避險需求略降溫，但聯準會維持限制性利率政策下，
美元仍具相對強勢基礎。惟隨市場逐步消化聯準會政策
路徑，美元進一步大幅升值空間有限，預估美元指數區
間約為101.9～103.2。(詳見Page 7、8)
 債市展望：企業盈利保持強勁，同時利率波動趨於平穩，
投等公司債供需環境有望改善，信用利差或進一步收窄。
留意高利率環境拉長下，部分高債務企業面臨再融資壓
力所引發的信用風險分化與降評。美國10年期公債殖利
率估在4.8%~5.3%。
 股市展望 : 隨著企業回購窗口重開、年底散戶資金流入
與選後外資買盤，可為受估值壓縮的股票帶來增量需求，
美股中長期仍具支撐。短期關注科技龍頭獲利指引與美
債殖利率走勢，若利率壓力趨緩，有助科技股與AI族群
延續上行。標普500指數預估在7,350～7,900點。

➢ Bloomberg預估美股2026年底目標價在8,000點，高
盛先前亦將年底標普500目標上調至8,000點，預計
2026年標普500 EPS達到340美元，年增24%，2027
年進一步成長至414美元。
➢ 機構提高指數目標的重要核心邏輯，是建立在企業獲
利預期明顯改善的基礎上。市場在意的是復甦是否會
變得更廣泛，而不是繼續集中在少數科技公司身上。
➢ 過去幾年投資者給予AI、半導體生產、雲計算和數據
中心基礎設施公司越來越高的估值。美國期中選舉後，
不是每家AI公司都面臨相同風險，也並非所有資本支
出減少就是負面的。而是進入一個市場變得更加挑剔
的階段，股價不再因公司擴大AI投資就給予獎勵，而
會越來越在乎這些投資是否帶來了合理回報。

### Page 5

美國製造業動能強勁，價格壓力稍緩解
5
10月ISM美國製造業指數為逾四年來次高
數據解讀
➢ 10月ISM美國製造業指數由9月的54.5升至55.5，優於
原估的54.5，為逾四年來次高。美國製造業已連續10個
月擴張，扭轉此前約三年的低迷走勢，工廠生產增速也
創近五年來最快。
➢ 進入第四季，美國製造業動能意外偏強，行業通膨壓力
略放緩，原物料價格指數略微回落至75.2。從10月ISM
數據來看，沒有跡象表明近期AI相關股票的波動是因景
氣活動走弱驅動。計算機及電子產品繼續擴張，AI和數
據中心供應鏈中的短缺持續存在。
➢ AI相關供應鏈仍然緊張，企業記憶體、集成電路、電子
元件和印刷電路板短缺，投資需求仍在擠壓可用供應。
需求提升幅度不及產出。新訂單小升，積壓訂單下降，
企業一定程度上依賴現有工作量，而非新訂單加速。
不過，客戶庫存指數仍穩居低位，這將支撐未來生產。
製造業生產加速就業崗位並未顯著增加

匯市表現說明
➢ 美元指數目前交投於102.88附近，11月初暫失動能，
原油價格下跌有助緩解通膨壓力，美元短期面臨阻力。
CME FedWatch數據顯示，12月升息的機率約為78%，
Fed偏應立場，則將有利於增強美元的價值。
➢ 年初更強的美國數據、Fed的穩定語氣以及地緣政治緊
張局勢都幫助支撐美元。澳幣/美元難以延續此前小幅
漲幅，因為美元維持看漲基調。
➢ 日圓維持弱勢震盪，在AI疑慮引發的股市拋售中，日圓
最初受益於避險需求。但日本家庭支出下滑抵消偏鷹派
的日本央行意見摘要，削弱日圓。此外，聯準會升息押
注支撐避險美元及USD/JPY貨幣對。
➢ 台灣10月CPI年率放緩至2.33%，低於預期，但仍高於
央行2%警戒門檻，10月PPI飆升19.7%，引發了對未來
消費者價格壓力的擔憂。外資從股市大量提款，央行出
手干預以支撐台幣，而外匯儲備降至5,964億美元。

美元趨勢訊號溫和看漲，但非極端狂熱
7
CFTC持續維持淨多頭支撐美元
圖表解讀
外
匯
市
場
資料來源： Bloomberg，11/09/2026
圖表解讀
➢ CFTC大型投機客持續維持美元淨多頭（約1.4萬口），
代表市場聰明錢對美元短中期走勢抱持謹慎樂觀的看
漲態度。投機客連續維持淨多單，顯示儘管市場面臨
宏觀經濟波動，資金仍將美元視為利差優勢或避險工
具的配置首選。
➢ 美元多頭續航力強，連續維持淨多頭，表示機構法人
與對沖基金的「多頭共識」尚未扭轉，美元指數下方
具強烈的支撐。但由於油價下跌有助緩解通膨，美元
短期面臨阻力。川普表示，在伊朗核計劃持續取得進
展且核姆茲海峽保持開放的前提下，進一步對伊朗採
取軍事行動“極不可能”，能源市場因此回落。
➢ 美元已不是極端看多，但仍屬偏多部位。相較2026年
夏季高峰，美元多頭已有部分獲利了結。若2027年
Fed維持「Higher for Longer」，美元部位仍有支撐。
若市場重新交易降息預期，淨多單有下降空間。
油價下跌緩解通膨，美元短線面臨阻力

基本面利多仍支撐新台幣匯率
8
圖表解讀
外
匯
市
場
資料來源：Bloomberg，11/03/2026
圖表解讀
➢ 新台幣在市場重新定價美台利率預期後，具潛在走強
與反彈空間。儘管Fed利率維持在3.75%～4.00%高檔，
美台利差擴大，加上外資賣股匯出的壓制，但台灣極
為強勁的經濟基本面與即將利空出盡的政策環境，將
成為支撐新台幣後市倒吃甘蔗的兩大核心動能。
➢ 台灣2026年3Q GDP年增率達到14.36%，連四季實現
兩位數成長，創近39年來最長連漲紀錄。這一增長進
一步拉高了12月台灣央行升息的預期。
➢ 在政策維持兩年多不變之後，台灣央行收緊政策的預
期有可能提前反映在隱含利率曲線上。三個月左右的
預期變化不大，但六個月時將上升約15個基點，接近
央行通常12.5個基點的收緊幅度。利率走升將縮減台
美利差，增加新台幣資產吸引力。

台韓市場隱含利率曲線預估

### Page 13

高利率不一定終結美股多頭
13
圖表解讀
➢ 利率上升不一定終結美股多頭，真正決定股市方向
的仍是企業獲利。目前美股仍處於「獲利成長大於
利率壓力」的環境，即便殖利率維持高檔，美股仍
有機會續創新高。多頭支撐來自企業資產負債表仍
健康，利息覆蓋率高，違約率低且現金充足，因此
目前不像2000年及2008年，尚未出現信用風險擴散。
➢ 10年期公債殖利率接近5%，美股還能漲嗎？答案
是可以，但空間受限。債券殖利率快速上升會對股
市造成短期壓力，但只要企業基本面穩健、融資成
本可控，殖利率上升未必演變成股市熊市。目前利
率水準不至於讓美股轉空，但壓縮評價面擴張空間，
報酬來源非PE擴張，轉向EPS成長。
➢ 當殖利率小幅上升（0~1個σ）時，標普500平均月
報酬約+0.5%~+1.5%，代表市場接受經濟成長帶
動的利率上升，當殖利率暴跌時（<-2σ），股市平
均 跌 約1% ， 反 映 經 濟 衰 退 擔 憂 ， 當 殖 利 率 暴 衝
（>2σ）時，名目殖利率急升，平均下跌約1%。資料來源：Bloomberg，10/23/2026

12月機會市場亮點
20

資產別 市場 亮點
股票
市場
美國 美股歷經利率與估值波動，但企業獲利維持穩健。大型科技企業持續受惠於雲端運算、AI、數位廣 告與企業軟體需求擴張，帶動標普500企業獲利維持上修，且選後不確定性降，為美股提供面支撐。
新亞 亞洲科技企業受惠AI需求強勁，獲利預估持續上修。台灣的晶圓代工、AI伺服器與高速傳輸供應鏈， 以及南韓的記憶體與高階晶片產業，皆具備全球競爭優勢，有望成未來數年獲利成長的主要來源。
台灣 生成式AI推動AI服務器出貨量增長和規格升級，利多半導體、光通訊、PCB等台灣領軍企業。年底 前法人、集團作帳及資金重新配置需求，加上市場對明年科技景氣展望轉趨樂觀，台股延續偏多。
科技/通訊 AI相關股票遭遇的拋售並不意味著市場出現全面崩盤，反有助於為此前過熱的AI投資熱潮降溫。AI 技術具有真實的商業價值，未來可創造可觀利潤，近期回調為投資者提供布局半導體股票的機會。
非必須
消費
美國就業市場與薪資仍具韌性，家庭資產負債表維持健康，房價與金融資產增值帶來財富效果，年

節零售旺季到來（黑色星期五、聖誕節），旅遊、娛樂、電商及高端品牌等非必需消費支出意願強。
工業 美國持續推動製造業回流與供應鏈重組，帶動半導體、電力設備、工業自動化、國防航太及基礎建 設相關投資。企業擴建產能與資料中心建設需求增加，為工業類股創造長期訂單與穩定成長機會。
債券
市場 投等債 升息後美國投等公司債殖利率來到近十多年相對高檔水準，可在兼顧信用品質下鎖定具吸引力的固 定收益。相較過去低利率時代，目前投資等級債提供更佳的收益緩衝，對長期資產配置具吸引力。
匯率
市場 美元 美國成長優勢、較高利率水準及AI資本支出循環持續吸引全球資金流入。通膨運行高於Fed 2%目 標且能源成本進一步上行風險下，穩健勞動力市場幾乎不給Fed鷹派放棄進一步收緊政策的理由。
看好減碼 中立

市場
歐洲 歐股評價仍處合理水準，具備一定的估值優勢。然而，歐元區經濟成長速度仍落後美國，企業投資 與消費復甦力道有限，使整體企業獲利成長幅度相對溫和，預期短期難有明顯領先全球股市的表現。
中國 中國經濟持續推出穩增長政策，但房地產調整、地方政府財政壓力及消費信心不足等問題仍未完全 改善。內需復甦速度不如市場預期，使企業營收與獲利成長面臨壓力，整體獲利上修動能相對有限。
新興
市場
新興市場經濟復甦漸受資金青睞，技術面偏多頭，其中新興亞股前景更為看好，主要受AI供應鏈驅
動，以及亞洲企業獲利成長支撐。亞洲經濟成長速度超過成熟市場，實質利率跌，提振股市表現。
健護 醫療健護設備股下調銷售前景壓抑類股走勢，較看好具規模優勢、研發能力及現金流的龍頭企業。
能源 全球原油供需大致維持平衡，雖然地緣政治風險持續存在，但全球經濟成長放緩與供給增加因素同 樣限制油價上行空間。在缺乏明確供需失衡情況下，能源股短期較難出現趨勢性行情。
公用
事業
高利率環境下，這類資本支出高、依賴浮動利率貸款的產業面臨直接的融資與利息成本壓力。不過，

AI 資料中心與科技基建引起的龐大電力、能源需求正在重塑基本面，建議偏防守與收益型配置。
房地產 美國房市活動維持區間震盪，受購屋負擔能力惡化限制，另方面則受長期住宅供給不足的支撐。進 入 2027 年後，隨著近期房貸利率再度攀升，負擔能力下降的逆風因素預計將逐漸主導市場走勢。
金融 利率維持相對高檔，有利銀行利息收入。資本市場活動、IPO與企業併購增加，投行財富務成長。
債券
市場
美國
公債
美國公債短期有利多，但非全面轉牛市。選舉後殖利率下滑，主要來自避險資金流入，預期Fed獨
立性提高，財政支出擴張機率下降，以及伊朗戰事升級風險下降
非投債 信用利差接近歷史低檔，市場對軟著陸與企業信用前景預期已反映在價格中，利差收窄空間有限。
新興債 部分新興國家仍面臨財政壓力、政治風險及外部融資需求挑戰，高收益伴隨波動，價值與風險並存。

• 期中選舉落幕，焦點重回企業獲利與經濟基本面，選
後不確定性下降有助風險偏好回升。
• AI基建投資持續擴張，帶動科技、工業及電力基建相
關企業獲利成長，成為支撐美股的重要力量。
• 債券收益率維持高檔，投等債兼具收益與防禦價值，
非投等債以息收為主要報酬來源。
• 台灣、南韓等新亞市場受惠企業獲利上修。
• 美國10年期公債殖利率仍是影響股市重要變數，但若維
持區間震盪，將有助科技股估值壓力緩解。

• 選後市場將重新檢視2027年企業獲利展望，科技龍頭
資本支出與AI變現能力仍是市場關注焦點。
• AI投資主軸由晶片擴散至電力設備、液冷散熱、網路基
礎設施及資料中心建設，受惠族群持續擴大。
• 若利率壓力進一步趨緩，有助全球股市延續年底行情。

## Document 721: 海外宏观周报：选后美股有望修复

Source PDF: all report pdf/海外宏观周报：选后美股有望修复.pdf
Report source: 中邮证券
Date: 2026-11-17
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

证券研究报告：宏观报告

市场有风险，投资需谨慎                                                        请务必阅读正文之后的免责条款部分 
研究所

分析师:李起 
SAC登记编号:S1340524110001 
Email:liqi2@cnpsec.com 
分析师:高晓洁 
SAC登记编号:S1340526020001 
Email:gaoxiaojie@cnpsec.com  
近期研究报告

《海外宏观周报：中期选举落定，科技
限制担忧或被高估》 - 2026.11.10   
 宏观观点

海外宏观周报：选后美股有望修复

⚫ 核心观点 
从历史表现看，中期选举后美股倾向于上涨。1950年以来，标普
500在中期选举投票日后12个月的平均价格涨幅为15.4%，高于非中
期选举年同期的 7.8%。近期，美国股票基金录得净流入，科技基金也
继续吸引资金，显示市场回调时仍有买盘承接。我们看好选后中期修
复，但当前不急于抄底。 
一方面，油价回落并未带动美债收益率下行，美元走强，金融条
件仍偏紧。沃勒在10月CPI公布前表示，若核心CPI环比仍在约 0.3%
或更高，12月可考虑加息50个基点。随后公布的核心 CPI环比上涨
0.3%，高于预期。我们仍预计12月加息25个基点。 在10月暂停加
息后迅速转向更大幅度的加息，容易使市场质疑政策节奏的一致性。
保尔森、哈玛克等大多数联储官员也倾向于渐进收紧。市场对明年继
续加息的预期正在升温，这将继续约束美股估值。 
另一方面，英伟达事件加大了国会对芯片出口合规的审查压力。
随着参众两院推进年度《国防授权法案》，《芯片安全法案》等出口管
制提案可能被纳入讨论。两党后续表态及条款变化仍可能加剧科技股
波动。我们倾向于待财报和政策前景进一步明朗后再增加配置，优先
关注盈利和现金流较稳健的科技龙头。

⚫ 风险提示： 
若核心通胀大幅上行，美联储 12月议息会议可能超预期鹰派，
科技股估值或进一步承压。若业绩指引超预期、芯片出口管制弱于市
场预期，科技股反弹可能提前启动。

发布时间：2026-11-17

### Page 4

请务必阅读正文之后的免责条款部分      4 
1 海外宏观事件与数据跟踪

美国通胀降温慢于预期，价格压力仍集中在多个分项。美国 10月 CPI环比
上涨0.4%，低于9月的 0.6%，但高于市场预期的 0.3%；核心CPI环比上涨0.3%，
与前两个月持平，高于市场预期的0.2%。住所价格涨幅有所放缓，但食品、电力、
娱乐和机动车保险等分项仍在上涨。由于去年同期政府停摆导致部分价格数据缺
失，美国劳工统计局未公布 10月整体及核心CPI同比增速。生产端方面，10月
PPI环比上涨 0.3%，低于预期的 0.4%，也低于上修后的前值 1.4%；但同比增速
升至6.5%，为 2022年11月以来最高。 
加息预期升温，美债收益率曲线趋平。核心 CPI高于预期，加上沃勒和洛根
公开讨论更快收紧的可能性，上周美债收益率普遍上行，短端涨幅大于长端。市
场开始重新评估下一次会议之后的收紧节奏，并提高了美联储在 12月及明年 1
月连续加息的定价。  
上周美股有所反弹，AI产业链内部继续分化。标普500指数全周上涨 2.5%，
纳斯达克 100指数上涨约 3.5%。存储、独立电力供应商和数据中心相关股票相
对占优，但长线资金仍在卖出英伟达，并转向部分大型云厂商和其他大型科技股。
CEO黄仁勋辞任的消息公布后，英伟达股价在后半周继续承压，标普 500指数周
五回落。 
上周国际油价回落，霍尔木兹海峡航运风险仍高。WTI原油期货价格回落至
88.56美元。市场对恢复大规模军事行动的担忧有所缓和。周六，伊朗国家电视
台称，三艘油轮在霍尔木兹海峡附近触雷起火。周日，伊朗革命卫队又声称袭击
了巴林一处美军基地的雷达系统。油价已从中期选举前的高位回落，但海上运输
风险仍存。

### Page 8

请务必阅读正文之后的免责条款部分      8 
2 美联储观点跟踪

1. 美联储理事沃勒： 
沃勒在10月 CPI公布前表示，若经济按预期发展，他倾向于在12月加息25
个基点；若核心CPI环比仍在0.3%或更高，则可考虑加息 50个基点。他还指出，
招聘和离职流动偏低，可能削弱劳动力与岗位的匹配效率，进而影响生产率。

2. 达拉斯联储主席洛根： 
洛根认为，通胀压力已扩展至能源以外的分项，目前的政策利率仍不足以对
经济形成适度约束。按照她对经济前景的判断，美联储至少还需要两次各 25个
基点的加息。

3. 美联储监管副主席鲍曼： 
鲍曼指出，能源成本和 AI投资相关需求正向核心商品和服务价格传导，劳
动力市场则出现趋稳迹象。她将关注 12月会议前公布的 PCE和就业报告，并评
估近期市场波动是否通过金融条件影响经济前景。

4. 费城联储主席保尔森： 
保尔森认为，通胀仍然偏高，进一步收紧政策是合适的。但考虑到工资上行
压力有限，通胀预期仍较稳定，她倾向于渐进加息。

5. 克利夫兰联储主席哈玛克： 
哈玛克指出，价格压力有所扩散，但目前没有必要转向更激进的加息。她倾
向于以较小幅度稳步调整，让家庭和企业有时间适应融资成本上升。

6. 纽约联储主席威廉姆斯： 
威廉姆斯表示，美国经济整体仍具韧性，美联储在降低通胀方面还有工作要
做，可能需要进一步加息。

### Page 9

请务必阅读正文之后的免责条款部分      9

7. 美联储副主席杰斐逊： 
杰斐逊指出，10月会议以来，美国经济活动和劳动力市场总体稳健，通胀走
势对下一步政策更有指示意义。他还表示，AI带来的生产率提升可能改变经济的
供给能力，但其对中性利率的影响仍难判断，政策应随数据趋势和风险变化调整。

8. 芝加哥联储主席古尔斯比（2027年票委）： 
古尔斯比认为，近几个月的价格压力已扩展至服务和医疗等黏性分项。此前
寄望于油价回落和关税影响消退带动通胀降温，但近期数据显示，尚未看到预期
中的通胀改善。

9. 旧金山联储主席戴利（2027年票委）： 
戴利倾向于进一步加息，尤其关注价格压力从能源和商品扩展至服务的迹象。
她认为，服务通胀更具黏性，但美联储仍有时间根据经济数据校准加息步伐。

10. 里士满联储主席巴尔金（2027年票委）： 
巴尔金认为，通胀仍是当前更突出的风险，企业裁员仍较少，对经济的影响
相对有限。面对持续偏高的通胀，美联储有理由采取行动，但应审慎把握调整幅
度和节奏。

图表9：市场预期今年 12月和明年 1月、3月各加息一次

公司简介 
中邮证券有限责任公司于2002年9月经中国证券监督管理委员会批准设立，公司注册资本61.68亿元人民币，
是中国邮政集团有限公司绝对控股的证券类金融子公司，公司是中邮创业基金管理股份有限公司的第二大股东。 
公司经营范围包括:证券经纪，证券自营，证券投资咨询，证券资产管理，融资融券，证券投资基金销售，证券
承销与保荐，代理销售金融产品，与证券交易、证券投资活动有关的财务顾问，具备展业的各项资格。截至 2025年
10月底，公司在全国设有 58家分支机构(含 29家分公司、29家营业部)，1家资产管理分公司和 1家另类投资子公
司。 
中邮证券紧密依托中国邮政集团有限公司的雄厚实力，通过强化“自营+协同”发展模式，实现快速发展，当前
服务的经纪客户已超过 260万人。公司始终坚持诚信经营、践行金融为民，为社会大众提供全方位专业化的证券投
融资服务，努力成为员工自豪、股东放心、客户信赖、社会尊重的优秀企业，打造契合中国邮政资源禀赋和市场地
位的特色精品券商。

## Document 741: 米中の覇権争いに揺れる中南米、「ドンロー主義」の行方はどうなる ～米中間の競争が続くなか、中間選挙後の米国の政策運営と中_d3452def
